| Agreement | The following TP for clause 8.1 of TS 38.213 is endorsed.<br>The corresponding CR for Rel-19 TS 38.213 in R1-2605098 is endorsed.<br>Change reason: The RO validation for adaptation fails to take partial overlap scenarios into account.<br>Change summary: Add the definition of partial overlap to the specification to align with the existing agreement.<br>Consequence if not approved: Under partial overlap conditions, the UE cannot correctly determine the validity of RO.<br><begin TP for subclause 8.1 of TS 38.213-j30><br>8.1	Random access preamble<br>Physical random access procedure for a UE is triggered upon request of a PRACH transmission by higher layers or by a PDCCH order or LTM Cell Switch Command MAC CE in clause 6.1.3.75 [11, TS 38.321] for a cell. A configuration by higher layers for a PRACH transmission includes the following:<br>-	A configuration for PRACH transmission on the cell [4, TS 38.211].<br>-	A preamble index, a preamble SCS, , a corresponding RA-RNTI when applicable [11, TS 38.321], and a PRACH resource for the cell.<br>-	A number of  preamble repetitions for the PRACH transmission if the UE would transmit the PRACH with repetitions.<br>A UE transmits a PRACH on a cell using the selected PRACH format with transmission power , as described in clause 7.4, on the indicated PRACH resource or on a determined set of  resources using a same spatial filter in case of  preamble repetitions.<br>For Type-1 random access procedure, a UE can be provided, by rach-Config-Adapt in RACH-ConfigCommon, parameters for determining time resources and frequency resources for PRACH transmission [4, TS 38.211]. When a PRACH occasion associated with rach-Config-Adapt overlaps in both time and frequency domain with has same frequency resource and same time resource as a PRACH occasion in RACH-ConfigCommon that is not associated with rach-Config-Adapt, the former PRACH occasion is not valid and is not considered in the procedures in this clause.<br><end TP for subclause 8.1 of TS 38.213-j30><br>NR-NTN | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605082](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605082.zip) |
| Agreement | The following TP for Clause 9 of TS 38.213 is endorsed.<br>The corresponding final CR for TS38.213 in R1-2605107 is endorsed.<br>\|  \|<br>\| Reason for change: For a UE enabled with inter-slot OCC operation, UE behaviour is not clear in case where a UE may determine to do multiplexing UCI in the PUSCH in a first slot and determine to drop the PUSCH in the second slot of a same OCC group. Summary of change: UE drops all the PUSCH repetitions in an OCC group before multiplexing UCI in the PUSCH when enabled with inter-slot OCC. Consequences if not approved: Unclear UE behaviour \|<br>\| 9 UE procedure for reporting control information *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** For the remaining of this clause, for a UE operating on an NTN serving cell, the timeline conditions for resolving time overlapping between a PUSCH transmission with repetitions in an OCC group [6, TS 38.214] and PUCCH transmissions are applicable with respect to the first repetition of the PUSCH transmission in the OCC group - if the UE would multiplex UCI from the PUCCH transmissions in the PUSCH, and for the case where the PUSCH would not be dropped as described in the remaining of this clause or in clause 6.1.2.1 [6, TS 38.214], the UE multiplexes the UCI in all repetitions of the PUSCH transmission in the OCC group - if the UE would not transmit a repetition of the PUSCH transmission, the UE does not transmit all repetitions of the PUSCH transmission in the OCC group - the UE does not expect to transmit in different slots more than one PUCCHs that provide HARQ-ACK information or CSI reports and would overlap with the PUSCH transmission in the OCC group *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604991](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604991.zip) |

| Agreement | With respect to the LS response to RAN4 on Collision Handling for HD-FDD VSATs is as follows, the following text is endorsed in principle.<br>From a RAN1 perspective, RAN1 has not identified any specific issue for re-using the HD-FDD (e)RedCap collision handling rules for HD-FDD VSAT UE. Existing collision handling rules to address (e)RedCap HD-FDD collision cases are band agnostic. RAN1 confirms that the (e)RedCap HD-FDD collision handling behaviors can be reused for HD-FDD VSAT UEs, without introducing new collision handling rules.<br>NOTE: RAN1 spec changes may be needed if RAN4 introduces a new HD-FDD VSAT UE capability. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605067](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605067.zip) |
| Agreement | The draft LS in R1-2605074 is endorsed with the following changes:<br>- In Action field: Change “agreements” to “agreement”.<br>- In heading: Add information for Response to field.<br>The final LS in R1-2605071 with applying above change is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605067](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605067.zip) |
| Agreement | Adopt the following TP to TS38.213 Section 11.1 in principle.<br>\| Title Draft CR on avoiding ambiguity for UE transmission in SBFD flexible symbols Reason for change There is an ambiguity between the UE and the gNB regarding PUCCH or PUSCH transmission in flexible symbols indicated as SBFD by tdd-UL-DL-ConfigurationCommon and associated with PRACH transmission in first PRACH occasions. In this case, the UE may assume the flexible symbols as SBFD symbols, whereas the gNB may schedule the PUCCH or PUSCH transmission assuming the flexible symbols as non-SBFD symbols. Such ambiguity leads to UE/gNB misalignment in the following transmission behaviours: Selection of p0AlphaSetforPUSCH or p0AlphaSetforPUSCH-SBFD in Clause 7 of TS38.213 Selection of p0AlphaSetforPUCCH or p0AlphaSetforPUCCH-SBFD in Clause 7 of TS38.213 Selection of msgA-Alpha or msgA-Alpha-sbfd in Clause 7.1.1 of TS38.213 Selection of p0-nominal or p0-nominal-sbfd in Clause 7.2.1 of TS38.213 Time domain resource allocation according to Clause 6.1.2.1 and 6.1.2.1a of TS38.214 Frequency hopping according to Clauses 6.3.1 and 6.3.3 of TS38.214 Summary of change For a PUCCH or a PUSCH transmission in flexible symbols indicated as SBFD by tdd-UL-DL-ConfigurationCommon and associated with PRACH transmission in first PRACH occasions, the UE assumes the flexible symbols as non-SBFD symbols. Consequences if not approved UE/gNB ambiguity for PUCCH or PUSCH transmission in SBFD flexible symbols associated with PRACH transmission in first PRACH occasions remains unresolved, leading to inconsistent transmission behaviour. \|<br>\| *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** 11.1 Slot configuration *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** A downlink or flexible symbol provided by tdd-UL-DL-ConfigurationCommon can include an UL sub-band provided by ul-SubbandlocationAndBandwidth, a first DL sub-band provided by firstDL-SubbandlocationAndBandwidth and may additionally include a second DL sub-band provided by secondDL-SubbandlocationAndBandwidth, for a SCS configuration of any configured UL BWP or DL BWP, respectively, as provided by scs-SpecificCarrierList [4, TS 38.211]. The downlink or flexible symbol is then referred to as an SBFD symbol; otherwise, it is referred to as a non-SBFD symbol. Unless otherwise stated, the UE considers symbols in a slot indicated as downlink and as SBFD by tdd-UL-DL-ConfigurationCommon to be available for transmissions. Uplink symbols by tdd-UL-DL-ConfigurationCommon are non-SBFD symbols. An SBFD symbol or a non-SBFD symbol provided by tdd-UL-DL-ConfigurationCommon cannot change to a non-SBFD symbol or to an SBFD symbol, respectively, by other information. The UE is not provided coresetPoolIndex and is not configured to receive PDSCH according to more than one TCI states mapped to one TCI codepoint [6, TS 38.214] for a serving cell where the UE is provided SBFD symbols. For a PUCCH or a PUSCH transmission in flexible symbols indicated as SBFD by tdd-UL-DL-ConfigurationCommon and associated with PRACH transmission in first PRACH occasions, the UE assumes the flexible symbols as non-SBFD symbols in this RACH attempt. *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** \|<br>Final CR in R1-2605127 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605085](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605085.zip) |
| Conclusion | When a UE determines a Type 1 power headroom report for a serving cell with SBFD configurations based on a reference PUSCH transmission, p0AlphaSetforPUSCH is used. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605066](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605066.zip) |
| Conclusion | The issue in section 3.2 in R1-2605066 can be avoided by NW configuration. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605066](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605066.zip) |
| Agreement | Adopt the following TP for 38.214 as alignment CR.<br>\| Reason for change: \| The parameter name ‘srs-ResourceSetToAddModListDCI-0-2-SBFD’ in Clause 6.2.1.5 is incorrect. \|<br>\|  \|  \|<br>\| Summary of change: \| Change ‘srs-ResourceSetToAddModListDCI-0-2-SBFD’ to ‘srs-ResourceSetToAddModListDCI-0-2’. \|<br>\|  \|  \|<br>\| Consequences if not approved: \| Incorrect parameter name \|<br>---------------------------------------------------------- Start of the text proposal ----------------------------------------------<br>6.2.1.5	UE sounding procedure for SBFD<br><unchanged texts omitted><br>For a same usage of 'codebook', 'noncodebook', or ‘beamManagement’, for the SRS resource sets provided in srs-ResourceSetToAddModList or srs-ResourceSetToAddModListDCI-0-2-SBFD, the number of SRS resources in a SRS resource set where symbolType is set to ‘sbfd’ is the same as the number of SRS resources in a SRS resource set where symbolType is set to ‘non-sbfd’.<br><unchanged texts omitted><br>---------------------------------------------------------- End of the text proposal ----------------------------------------------<br>LP-WUS | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605066](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605066.zip) |
| Conclusion | If at least one OFDM symbol in the WUS monitoring occasion overlaps with active time or DTX inactive period for the primary cell, a UE may not monitor WUS.<br>- No spec change is needed<br>Mobility Phase 4 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605077](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605077.zip) |
| Agreement | Adopt the following TP to TS38.214 Section 5.2.4a<br>\| Reason for change \| For LTM, ‘ltm-CandidateConfig’ includes an RRCReconfiguration message that is used to provide an LTM candidate configuration, where the RRCReconfiguration message includes a ‘CellGroupConfig’ containing a ‘ReconfigurationWithSync’ that carries a ‘newUE-Identity’ to indicate a RNTI value for UE applied in target cell. For L3 handover, such ‘newUE-Identity’ is also provided for UE through handover command ‘ReconfigurationWithSync’. Thus, for 2-step CBRA based LTM or handover, UE always carries the C-RNTI in MSG A. Based on the above considerations and the CR (R1-2603427) already agreed in RAN1#124bis meeting, the wording “MSG B or” in clause 5.2.4a of TS 38.214-j30 should be removed. The reason is that for the determination of first PUSCH for early CSI report in 2-step CBRA based LTM or handover, the UE shall transmit the CSI report to the candidate cell using the first PUSCH corresponding to a dynamic grant or a configured grant after the HARQ-ACK transmission corresponding to “PDSCH scheduled by a PDCCH transmission addressed to the C-RNTI that is received in response to MSG A”, rather than referencing “MSG B”. \|<br>\| Summary of change \| Remove “MSG B or” from the following paragraph in clause 5.2.4a of TS 38.214-j30 with updates based on the endorsed CR (R1-2603427). \|<br>\| Consequences if not approved \| The UE behavior on determining UL resource of early CSI report when C-RNTI has been included in MSG A for CBRA based LTM or handover scenario is incorrect. \|<br>\| Clauses affected \| 5.2.4a \|<br>\| 5.2.4a CSI Reporting for LTM and handover *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** For RACH-less LTM cell switch or RACH-less handover [23, TS 38.300], the UE shall transmit the CSI report to the candidate cell using the first PUSCH corresponding to a dynamic grant or a configured grant [6, TS 38.213]. For RACH-based LTM cell switch or RACH-based handover using a contention-free random access procedure [23, TS 38.300], the UE shall transmit the CSI report to the candidate cell using the PUSCH scheduled by the RAR UL grant or MsgA PUSCH. For RACH-based LTM cell switch or RACH-based handover using a contention-based random access procedure [23, TS 38.300], the UE shall transmit the CSI report to the candidate cell using the first PUSCH corresponding to a dynamic grant or a configured grant after the HARQ-ACK transmission corresponding to , or the first PUSCH scheduled by a PDCCH transmission addressed to the C-RNTI, received in response to Msg3. *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** \| 5.2.4a CSI Reporting for LTM and handover *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** For RACH-less LTM cell switch or RACH-less handover [23, TS 38.300], the UE shall transmit the CSI report to the candidate cell using the first PUSCH corresponding to a dynamic grant or a configured grant [6, TS 38.213]. For RACH-based LTM cell switch or RACH-based handover using a contention-free random access procedure [23, TS 38.300], the UE shall transmit the CSI report to the candidate cell using the PUSCH scheduled by the RAR UL grant or MsgA PUSCH. For RACH-based LTM cell switch or RACH-based handover using a contention-based random access procedure [23, TS 38.300], the UE shall transmit the CSI report to the candidate cell using the first PUSCH corresponding to a dynamic grant or a configured grant after the HARQ-ACK transmission corresponding to , or the first PUSCH scheduled by a PDCCH transmission addressed to the C-RNTI, received in response to Msg3. *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** \|<br>Final CR in R1-2605090 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605008](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605008.zip) |
| Agreement | Adopt the following TP to TS38.215 Section 5.1.1<br>\| Reason for change: \| CSI-RS-based intra-frequency L1-RSRP measurement and inter-frequency L1-RSRP measurement for neighbor cell have been supported for LTM, but only CSI-RS-based intra-frequency L1-RSRP measurement is captured in TS38.215. Besides, in the last meeting, CR on adding the inter-frequency scenario to applicable scenarios of SS-RSRP for R18 LTM was agreed. But the corresponding description in the R19 specification has not been revised. \|<br>\| Summary of change \| Capture CSI-RS-based and SSB-based inter-frequency L1-RSRP measurement for neighbor cell in Rel-19 LTM. \|<br>\| Consequences if not approved \| CSI-RS-based inter-frequency L1-RSRP measurement and SSB-based inter-frequency L1-RSRP measurement for neighbor cell in Rel-19 LTM are not supported for Rel-19 LTM. \|<br>\| Clauses affected \| 5.1.1 \|<br>---------------------------------------------Start of TP#3-2-------------------------------------------------------------<br>5.1.1	SS reference signal received power (SS-RSRP)<br>\| Definition \| SS reference signal received power (SS-RSRP) is defined as the linear average over the power contributions (in [W]) of the resource elements that carry secondary synchronization signals. The measurement time resource(s) for SS-RSRP are confined within SS/PBCH Block Measurement Time Configuration (SMTC) window duration, except when measurement is being performed by wake-up receiver (WUR). If SS-RSRP is used for L1-RSRP as configured by reporting configurations as defined in TS 38.214 [6], the measurement time resources(s) restriction by SMTC window duration is not applicable. For SS-RSRP determination demodulation reference signals for physical broadcast channel (PBCH) and, if indicated by higher layers, CSI reference signals in addition to secondary synchronization signals may be used. SS-RSRP using demodulation reference signal for PBCH or CSI reference signal shall be measured by linear averaging over the power contributions of the resource elements that carry corresponding reference signals taking into account power scaling for the reference signals as defined in TS 38.213 [5]. If SS-RSRP is not used for L1-RSRP, the additional use of CSI reference signals for SS-RSRP determination is not applicable. SS-RSRP shall be measured only among the reference signals corresponding to SS/PBCH blocks with the same SS/PBCH block index and the same physical-layer cell identity. If SS-RSRP is not used for L1-RSRP and higher-layers indicate certain SS/PBCH blocks for performing SS-RSRP measurements, then SS-RSRP is measured only from the indicated set of SS/PBCH block(s). For frequency range 1, the reference point for the SS-RSRP shall be the antenna connector of the UE. For frequency range 2, SS-RSRP shall be measured based on the combined signal from antenna elements corresponding to a given receiver branch. For frequency range 1 and 2, if receiver diversity is in use by the UE, the reported SS-RSRP value shall not be lower than the corresponding SS-RSRP of any of the individual receiver branches. \|<br>\| Applicable for \| If SS-RSRP is used for L1-RSRP, RRC_CONNECTED intra-frequency, If SS-RSRP is being performed by wake-up receiver (WUR), RRC_IDLE for serving cell, RRC_INACTIVE for serving cell. Otherwise, RRC_IDLE intra-frequency, RRC_IDLE inter-frequency, RRC_INACTIVE intra-frequency, RRC_INACTIVE inter-frequency, RRC_CONNECTED intra-frequency, RRC_CONNECTED inter-frequency \|<br>5.1.2	CSI reference signal received power (CSI-RSRP)<br>\| Definition \| CSI reference signal received power (CSI-RSRP), is defined as the linear average over the power contributions (in [W]) of the resource elements of the antenna port(s) that carry CSI reference signals configured for RSRP measurements within the considered measurement frequency bandwidth in the configured CSI-RS occasions. For CSI-RSRP determination CSI reference signals transmitted on antenna port 3000 according to TS 38.211 [3] shall be used. If CSI-RSRP is used for L1-RSRP, CSI reference signals transmitted on antenna ports 3000, 3001 can be used for CSI-RSRP determination. For intra-frequency CSI-RSRP measurements, if the measurement gap is not configured, UE is not expected to measure the CSI-RS resource(s) outside of the active downlink bandwidth part. For frequency range 1, the reference point for the CSI-RSRP shall be the antenna connector of the UE. For frequency range 2, CSI-RSRP shall be measured based on the combined signal from antenna elements corresponding to a given receiver branch. For frequency range 1 and 2, if receiver diversity is in use by the UE, the reported CSI-RSRP value shall not be lower than the corresponding CSI-RSRP of any of the individual receiver branches. \|<br>\| Applicable for \| If CSI-RSRP is used for L1-RSRP, RRC_CONNECTED intra-frequency, Otherwise, RRC_CONNECTED intra-frequency, RRC_CONNECTED inter-frequency \|<br>Final CR in R1-2605091 is endorsed.<br>7-24 GHz | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605008](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605008.zip) |
| Agreement | TP and cover page contents in R1-2603895 for TR 38.901 are agreed in principle.<br>Final CR in R1-2605075 is endorsed.<br>ISAC | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603895](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603895.zip) |

| Conclusion | - From RAN1 perspective, WID revision is needed for Rel-20 LBCA via switching, to include at least<br>  - TS38.213 as impacted TS<br>- Companies are encouraged to submit contributions to RAN#112 (June’26) for WID revision proposal | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604981](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604981.zip) |

## CSI Acquisition / Prediction

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For the study of non-AI/AI based CSI prediction in frequency/spatial domain at UE side, consider the following aspects<br>- Prediction input (UE side Input): reduced CSI-RS measurement<br>- Additional information to be used by UE side for CSI prediction, if applicable<br>  - Option 1: Long-term channel information derived from full-dimensional CSI-RS measurement<br>  - Option 2: Reconstruction information (indicated by BS) applied on the measured channel based on non-precoded CSI-RS,<br>  - Option 3: Reconstruction information based on precoded CSI-RS (indicated by BS), e.g., precoder applied on CSI-RS<br>  - Option 4: DMRS based CSI measurement<br>  - Other options or combination of above options are not precluded<br>  - Companies are encouraged to study whether there is performance impact caused by aging/temporary unavailability of the additional information<br>- Prediction output (UE side Output): Predicted CSI to be used for CSI report<br>  - Note: UE side Output does not necessarily equal the CSI report quantity<br>  - Companies to report the details of the predicted CSI for CSI report | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the study on configurable/downloadable codebook/basis/bases, consider at least the following:<br>- The information for NW to determine the configurable/downloadable codebook/basis/bases, e.g., reported CPI, SRS, previous reported CSI<br>  - Companies report the detail assumptions for obtaining such information in the evaluation, at least including<br>    - Additional signaling overhead for information collection, if applicable<br>    - The assumptions of the information, e.g., the SRS related assumption, CPI reporting related assumption, time domain relationship with the target CSI report<br>    - Additional measurement overhead, if applicable<br>- Companies report<br>  - BS antenna configuration, subband configuration<br>  - signalling overhead, representing format of configurable/downloadable basis/codebook, updated frequency of the configurable/downloadable basis or configurable/downloadable codebook, how to determinate when to update<br>  - whether the configurable/downloadable codebook or basis is UE- specific or UE-common (Cell-specific), rank/layer- common or specific<br>  - codebook structure assumption for configurable/downloadable codebook/basis, e.g., eType-II-like, Type-I-like<br>  - complexity at UE side for CSI feedback with configurable/ downloadable basis/codebook for CSI feedback: e.g., number of complex operations for CSI computation and memory requirement for basis storage<br>  - whether NW-sided model is assumed<br>    - If NW-sided model is assumed, companies report the input/output, labeling.<br>- UE speed assumption follows the general EVM section 10.1 and other EVM follows the assumption agreed in RAN1#124. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For study of DMRS-based CSI, considering at least the following aspects:<br>- Potential use cases, including e.g., increased CSI accuracy including inter-layer imbalance information, CSI-RS overhead reduction, CSI acquisition for TDD.<br>- Benchmark:<br>  - CSI-RS based<br>    - P CSI-RS and/or AP CSI-RS based (precoded or non-precoded)<br>  - SRS based<br>- Company to report CSI-RS/SRS assumption for benchmark<br>- Company to report the assumptions for DMRS resources and how DMRS is used for CSI measurement, e.g., only for channel measurement or both channel and interference measurement.<br>- Companies to report the OLLA assumption details, if applicable<br>- KPI:<br>  - Throughput (average and edge UPT) vs total CSI feedback overhead (averaged over UEs, over simulation time (per time unit)), where the total CSI feedback overhead includes both DMRS based feedback, and other necessary CSI feedback (if applicable)<br>  - Note: DL RS overhead is included in throughput calculation.<br>- DMRS based report quantity:<br>  - at least CQI, companies report CQI calculation assumption<br>  - companies can report other quantities, e.g., PMI (e.g., refinement), RI, RSRP/SINR, DMRS port selection indication if assumed, including the detailed assumption<br>- UE speed assumption follows the general EVM section 10.1 and other EVM follows the assumption agreed in RAN1#124. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Support at least codebook based implicit CSI feedback in 6GR.<br>Study whether/how to support explicit CSI feedback, including at least the following aspects:<br>- Reporting quantities/format, including compression/quantization methods<br>  - e.g., full channel matrix, channel covariance matrix, eigenvector(s) with eigenvalue(s)<br>- Companies to provide reporting overhead, instance assumption for channel covariance matrix, performance and complexity analysis, and assumption for link adaptation and other reference signal (if applicable)<br>- Note: both sTRP and mTRP can be considered<br>- Note: Both AI and non-AI based solution for CSI feedback can be considered.<br>- Note: The related testability issue is up to RAN 4.<br>- Note: LS to RAN 4 is to be considered for RAN 4 testability issues, when mature. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | At least support NW triggered and/or configured CSI reporting.<br>Study whether/how to support UE-initiated or event-triggered CSI reporting, at least the following aspects:<br>- Performance and complexity evaluation, including signalling overhead<br>  - Taking NW triggered and/or configured CSI as benchmark<br>- Reporting format/quantities<br>- Event definition including use cases/scenarios and triggering and reporting mechanism<br>  - e.g., CSI report/component is transmitted only if associated event has occurred, CSI reporting configuration selection/adaptation by the UE if associated event has occurred<br>- Note: UE-initiated (event-triggered) reporting for beam management is to be discussed separately in the Beam Management agenda | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the 6GR codebook design for CSI report, the following is considered:<br>- NR fixed DFT basis is used as a baseline for evaluation for W1 and Wf  at least for non-precoded CSI-RS<br>  - For cell-specific precoded CSI-RS, port-selection basis and/or NR DFT basis can be used as a baseline for evaluation<br>  - FFS on other basis, e.g., for near field, basis combined from DFT beams/port selection, and non-orthogonal DFT basis<br>- Low- resolution scheme:<br>  - Max RI=8 as a baseline for evaluation<br>  - Candidate structures include Type-I Scheme-A like (W1 W2), Type-I Scheme-B like (W1 W2), eType-II like L=1 (W1 W2 Wf) structures<br>    - For eType-II like L=1, layer-specific/layer-group-specific SD basis selection<br>    - Other candidate schemes are not precluded<br>  - FFS: details on whether/how to accommodate passive array with smaller number of ports<br>- High- resolution scheme:<br>  - Max RI=4 as a baseline for evaluation<br>  - eType-II L>1 (W1 W2 Wf) structure as a baseline for evaluation (including implicit and explicit feedback)<br>  - Note: Whether/how to use this for explicit channel feedback is not precluded.<br>- For both low- and high-res, CJT codebook with NTRP>1 TRPs/panels<br>  - TRP/panel specific SD basis selection, TRP/panel common FD basis selection (if applicable) as a baseline for evaluation<br>  - Study whether/how to support dynamic TRP/panel selection mechanism<br>  - Company reports the multi-TRP/panel assumption, e.g., co-located, non-co-located<br>  - Note: The term “TRP” and “panel” are used for discussion purposes<br>Note: Additional details are still to be studied, including enhancements for UPT-overhead trade-off and UE complexity | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the study of non-AI/AI based CSI prediction in frequency/spatial domain at NW side, consider the following aspects<br>- Prediction input:<br>  - Option 1-1: UE reported channel matrix derived from reduced dimension CSI-RS measurement<br>  - Option 1-2: UE reported coefficient matrix, which is based on the multiplication of a downloaded matrix and the channel matrix derived from reduced dimension CSI-RS measurement<br>  - Option 1-3: UE reported precoding matrix derived from reduced dimension CSI-RS measurement<br>  - Option 1-4: UE reported coefficient matrix, which is based on the multiplication of a downloaded matrix and the precoding matrix derived from reduced dimension CSI-RS measurement<br>  - Other options are not precluded<br>  - Note: Performance impact caused by quantization of the UE reported CSI is part of the study<br>- Additional information to be used by NW side for CSI prediction, if applicable<br>  - Option 2-1: Long-term channel information corresponding to full dimension CSI reported by UE<br>  - Option 2-2: Channel information corresponding to full dimension CSI derived from UL measurement<br>  - Option 2-3: Channel measurement based on DL DMRS reported by UE<br>  - For spatial domain, “dimension” is defined in terms of ports<br>  - For frequency domain, companies to report the assumed granularity to define “full dimension”<br>- Prediction output: Full-dimension CSI to be used by BS<br>  - Note: companies are encouraged to report the details of the full-dimension CSI<br>- Note: companies to report the assumption of rank and MCS determination in the simulation | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For RE locations of CDM groups in 6GR CSI-RS pattern design, support Option 1-1: Localized CDM group similar to 5G NR, where one CDM group contains adjacent REs. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For OCC sequence used in a 6GR CSI-RS CDM group, study the following options<br>- Option 1: Walsh sequence<br>- Option 2: DFT sequence | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the study of non-AI/AI based CSI prediction in time domain at UE side, consider the following aspects<br>- Prediction input: RS measurement from one or multiple occasions in the observation window<br>  - The RS includes at least CSI-RSs<br>  - Study the impact of RS with longer periodicity (e.g., 10ms, 20ms, for evaluation), and potential solutions, if needed<br>- Study whether additional information is beneficial for UE side time domain CSI prediction, e.g., DMRS based measurement, IMR<br>- Prediction output: CSI in the prediction window to be reported by UE<br>  - The CSI includes at least channel prediction, study whether/how to enable interference prediction<br>  - Study whether to support both N4=1 and N4>1 in the prediction window, and associated UE reporting mechanism<br>    - The definition of N4 is same as NR<br>- NR Rel-18/Rel-19 UE side CSI prediction as a start point<br>  - Study the extension to >32 ports<br>- Note: At least for CSI prediction without interference prediction, UE side time domain CSI prediction is to be compared with NW side time domain CSI prediction | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the study of 6GR CSI-RS pattern in single or multiple slots/RBs in time/frequency domain<br>- The number of slots for defining CSI-RS pattern is M1, and the number of RBs for defining CSI-RS pattern is M2. Study the following further aspects in this case<br>  - For RE location in the M1 slots and M2 RBs,<br>    - Option 1-1: Restrict that CSI-RS pattern occupies a same set of REs in each of the M1 slots and M2 RBs<br>    - Option 1-2: CSI-RS pattern can occupy same or different set of REs in each of the M1 slots and M2 RBs<br>      - The number of REs occupied by CSI-RS in the M1 slots and M2 RBs can be same or different<br>    - FFS whether the set of REs occupied by CSI-RS changes across occasions<br>  - For mapping order of CDM groups in the M1 slots and M2 RBs<br>    - Option 2-1: Mapping order of CDM groups in the M1 slots and M2 RBs is fixed across multiple occasions/symbols<br>    - Option 2-2: Mapping order of CDM groups in the M1 slots and M2 RBs is switched across multiple occasions/symbols<br>  - Whether both or which one from M1 and M2 can be larger than 1 is to be further studied<br>  - Study details for M1 and M2, including both the upper bound and detailed values<br>- Note: for RAN1 study purpose, whether the CSI-RS pattern mentioned above is from NW or UE perspective is a separate discussion | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For CSI-RS sharing use case 1 (sharing a common set or subset of REs among CSI-RS with different number of ports) in 6GR, study the following aspects<br>- Level 1: Sharing granularity is CDM group level<br>- Level 2: Sharing granularity can be smaller than a CDM group | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For CSI-RS sharing use case 2 (Sharing a common set or subset of REs among CSI-RS with different frequency densities), study whether/how to achieve frequency density common sequence mapping. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR CSI container design, CSI report in L1 is supported with<br>- separate CSI measurement/computation triggering/configuration and CSI reporting triggering/configuration<br>- minimizing collision handling cases and simplification on the collision handling rules between more than 1 UL channels where at least 1 of the UL channels carrying CSI reports,<br>  - E.g., No collision handling rules between multiple carriers, if applicable<br>  - Designs are to be discussed and decided in 10.5.4.4<br>- streamlined CSI timeline including timeline relaxation | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For CJT calibration report,<br>- Study the following three types of reporting quantities:<br>- Inter-TRP (xTRP) delay offset, analogous to Rel-19 cjtc-Dd<br>- Inter-TRP (xTRP) frequency offset, analogous to Rel-19 cjtc-F<br>- Inter-TRP (xTRP) DL/UL phase offset, analogous to Rel-19 cjtc-P<br>- Note: Additional details are still to be studied, including dynamic range and resolution, and their additional use cases (if any)<br>- Stand-alone reporting as a baseline for evaluation<br>- Study whether/how to support reporting CJTC delay offset report and codebook-based CSI (with NTRP>1)<br>- Note: when CSI-RS and DMRS have different delay pre-compensation assumption, the potential impact on DMRS based channel estimation can be discussed in 10.5.2.2 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the study on channel property information (CPI), the evaluation at least considering the following:<br>Category of use cases:<br>- CSI configuration/report adaptation for overhead/complexity reduction, CSI feedback with more accuracy, and/or CSI prediction<br>- Reference signal configuration adaptation (e.g., CSI-RS for CSI, DMRS, SRS)<br>- DL/UL transmission scheduling and configuration (e.g., coordinated beamforming/scheduling for inter-TRP/cell/user interference suppression)<br>- Note: the above categories can be applicable to sTRP/mTRP.<br>Basic categories of channel properties<br>- Spatial-domain (SD)<br>- Frequency-domain (FD)<br>- Time-domain (TD)<br>- Any combination of the above domains<br>Measurement definition and/or reporting formats, for example:<br>- Correlation information (e.g., time-/frequency/spatial-domain correlation)<br>- Spectrum (e.g., channel statistics in a transform domain, basis indices and power)<br>- Channel covariance matrix<br>- Explicit channel matrix<br>- Explicit system parameters (e.g. codebook parameters, RS parameters)<br>- Other formats are not precluded.<br>Note: Strive for common report quantities for use cases, if applicable.<br>Note: The related testability issue is up to RAN 4.<br>Note: LS to RAN 4 is to be considered for RAN 4 testability issues, when mature. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For SLS/LLS evaluation for JSCM-based CSI compression,<br>- For both JSCM and SSCCM, the following Tx/Rx chain is considered.<br>Companies report the assumption and/or abstraction for each block. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For SLS evaluation for JSCM-based CSI compression,<br>- Considering the following options of the SSCCM below could be used for the SLS evaluation as a starting point:<br>  - Option 1: variable RE overhead {Throughput vs CSI feedback overhead (# of REs)}<br>    - Step 1: for SSCCM, fixed payload determination (per PC or per payload)<br>      - Different parameter combination or payload is simulated.<br>    - Step 2: CSI feedback overhead (# of REs) is averaged of scheduled UEs in SLS, and the UL resource of each UE is determined based on the UL SINR considering link adaption<br>    - Step 3(if applicable): check whether UL transmission is successfully decoded or not<br>      - Based on the PHY abstraction (flip coin) according to UL SINR for each UE<br>        - Y: Calculate UPT based on the current CSI<br>        - N: Calculate UPT based on the historical CSI<br>    - Step 4: gNB uses the received CSI feedback for DL transmission for UPT calculation across UEs.<br>  - Option 2: Fixed CSI feedback overhead (#RE) with per UE level PC/payload selection for SSCCM and per scheduling UE level uplink link adaptation for SSCCM<br>    - Step 1: Fixed CSI feedback overhead (# of REs) is used across all UEs.<br>      - Payload determination (parameter combination/payload) for each UE<br>        - Companies report how to determine the parameter commination/payload<br>      - for each UE, the payload size can be selected according to UL SINR<br>        - Companies report how to generate UL SINR, e.g., based on geometry only, or based on geometry and fast fading.<br>        - Companies report the target BLER and UL SINR definition (e.g., port domain or UL SINR after Rx combination)<br>    - Step 2 (if applicable): check whether UL transmission is successfully decoded or not<br>      - Based on the PHY abstraction (flip coin) according to UL SINR for each UE<br>        - Y: Calculate UPT based on the current CSI and update the historical CSI<br>        - N: Calculate UPT based on the historical CSI<br>  - Option 3: variable RE overhead with per scheduled UE level PC/payload selection and per scheduling UE level uplink link adaptation for SSCCM<br>    - Step 1:<br>      - Payload determination (parameter combination/payload) and link adaptation to determine the variable RE for each UE<br>        - Companies report how to determine the parameter combination/payload and link adaption<br>    - Step 2 (if applicable): check whether UL transmission is successfully decoded or not<br>      - Based on the PHY abstraction (flip coin) according to UL SINR for each UE<br>        - Y: Calculate UPT based on the current CSI and update the historical CSI<br>        - N: Calculate UPT based on the historical CSI<br>        - Note: Error free can be reported.<br>    - Step 3: gNB uses the received CSI feedback for DL transmission for UPT calculation across UEs<br>  - Other refinement to the above options are not precluded.<br>  - Other options can be reported.<br>- SSCCM baseline assumption for SLS is up to companies’ report<br>- For JSCM, CSI feedback overhead is selected for each scheduled UEs in SLS.<br>  - Companies report how to select the CSI overhead for each UE for other method.<br>- Companies report how to generate UL SINR, e.g., based on geometry only, or based on geometry and fast fading.<br>  - Companies report the uplink channel estimation error assumption.<br>- Companies report the target BLER (e.g., error free, or 1% or 10%) and UL SINR definition (e.g., port domain or UL SINR after Rx combination) for SSCCM in Step 2.<br>- Fixed max Tx power for uplink, companies report MPR assumption in the simulation. Companies report the UL power control assumption, e.g., P0 and alpha values.<br>  - PRB is used as power control adjustment granularity<br>- Companies report constellation assumption and corresponding PAPR (considering potential multiplexing with other UCI/UL-SCH).<br>- 1Tx or multiple Tx can be considered for UL. If more than 1 Tx is assumed, companies report the UL Tx/precoder assumptions.<br>- Companies report the PHY abstraction for uplink based on polar coding, if applicable.<br>- Companies report the handling of scalability of number of REs for JSCM-based CSI compression, if applicable, and whether to adopt UCI only on PUSCH or UCI with UL-SCH for benchmark and JSCM-based compression.<br>- Companies report the assumptions of JSCM, e.g., the projection-matrix-based compression (compress target CSI by multiplying with a projection matrix at UE) or encoder-based (e.g., transformer-based)<br>- Companies report the assumption on obtaining projection matrix/encoder for JSCM-based CSI compression<br>- Companies report the assumption on DL CSI-RS channel estimation error:<br>  - Error modelling in TR36.897 Table A.1-2 as a baseline if channel estimation error is modelled. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Study the following cases for AI/ML UL-based CSI, and strive to support the unified SRS patterns/designs/framework that work for both AI/ML and non-AI/ML schemes.<br>- AI/ML for low-overhead SRS (Sub-Case A): low-overhead SRS with AI/ML at the network side for CSI reconstruction<br>  - Note: low-overhead SRS may include overhead reduction schemes in the time and/or frequency domains. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the evaluation of AI/ML-based UL-based CSI acquisition,<br>  - Support to reuse the assumption for evaluation of UL-based CSI<br>  - FLOPs and number of parameters of AI/ML model are reported by company | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the evaluation of scalability/generalization of AI/ML-based UL-based CSI acquisition, the evaluation methodology in NR (as in TR 38.843) can be considered as the starting point. The set of configurations are considered focusing on one or more of the following aspects:<br>- Various antenna configurations, e.g., UE Tx port number, UE Tx port layout<br>- Various channel and link conditions, e.g., SNR/SINR ranges<br>- Various time/frequency domain factors, e.g., various frequency allocations/RPFS/hopping patterns, time domain allocations, UE speeds<br>- Other aspects, e.g., power of SRS transmission, deployment scenarios, channel models<br>Note: Companies are encouraged to evaluate generalization/scalability performance across multiple scenario/configuration combinations. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Consider SRS repetition and cross-slot SRS transmission mechanisms specified in NR as a starting point for 6GR. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For multiplexing of multiple SRS ports, study at least the following options:<br>  - TDM<br>  - CDM in frequency domains (at least include CS)<br>  - TD-OCC<br>  - FDM (at least include comb and comb offset)<br>  - Combinations of above options<br>  - Note: other schemes are not precluded<br>- Strive for a unified design framework for different numbers of ports.<br>- FFS whether/how to support dynamic/flexible adaptation of multiplexing parameters(e.g., comb and CS).<br>  - Whether/how dynamic/flexible adaptation of multiplexing parameters can be achieved beyond resource/resource-set level indication can be discussed separately | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Study at least the following aspects of SRS antenna switching for DL CSI acquisition<br>- Guard Period (GP) between consecutive SRS resources for antenna switching<br>- Whether and how to define the GP between consecutive SRS resources for antenna switching, considering the practical RF chain switching times of UEs<br>- GP between SRS and other proceeding/following UL signal/channel transmission, if different antennas are used for SRS and other proceeding/following UL signal/channel transmission,<br>- Whether/how to define GP between SRS and other proceeding/following UL signal/channel transmission<br>- Allowed combinations of x (number of Tx antennas at UE) and y (number of Rx antennas at UE)<br>- Impact of realistic UE antenna layouts (both symmetric and asymmetric number of antenna ports across RF chains)<br>- Whether/how to handle power imbalance among SRS antenna ports due to insertion loss<br>Note: regarding the practical RF chain switching times of UEs, and power imbalance, studies/conclusions from RAN4 should be considered by RAN1 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For UL-based CSI in 6GR, study carrier switching and consider at least the following aspects<br>- Take SRS carrier switching mechanisms in NR as the starting point for 6GR carrier switching study.<br>- Strive to simplify the SRS carrier switching framework compared to NR, e.g.,<br>  - By converging the redundant triggering alternatives to reduce UE complexity and standardization overhead<br>  - By simplifying the collision handling rules when SRS for carrier switching collides with other UL signals/channels<br>  - By reducing/eliminating unnecessary switching-back | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR SRS sequence design,<br>  - candidate options including:<br>  - ZC and CGS sequences in NR<br>  - Enhanced ZC<br>  - Enhanced CGS<br>  - other directions are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | - For SRS transmission procedure to facilitate sounding in the UL of all parts of the DL bandwidth where the DL CBW is larger than supported UL CBW, study at least the following aspects/solutions<br>- UL sounding for all parts of the target bandwidth over multiple transmissions of SRS, at least consider the following for solutions<br>  - with a single SRS resource<br>  - with multiple SRS resources<br>- Consider the impacts on other signals/channels (including at least interruption of data transmission, collision handling), required number of switching, switching gap, SRS overhead to sound all parts of the DL bandwidth, time to complete the sounding<br>- Accommodating any location of UL BW, if supported<br>- Other aspects/solutions are not excluded<br>Note: studies/conclusions from RAN4 might be needed. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Study and evaluate the following options on the candidate RS(s) for finer time and frequency tracking for connected and/or idle mode, if applicable<br>- Option 1: Dedicated RS for finer time and frequency tracking<br>  - Periodic/semi-persistent RS, standalone aperiodic RS or combination of them can be considered for finer timing and frequency tracking<br>- Option 2: DMRS<br>  - PDSCH DMRS, PDCCH DMRS can be considered<br>- Option 3: Synchronization signals<br>  - SSB and other types of signals/channel structure or reference signal, if applicable<br>- Combination of different options can also be considered, e.g., SS + dedicated RS for finer time and frequency tracking, dedicated RS for finer time and frequency tracking + DMRS, SS+DMRS etc.<br>- Note 1: Periodic TRS in 5G NR is taken as the baseline for evaluation.<br>- Note 2: For comprehensive comparison, companies are encouraged to provide simulation results at least considering the following assumptions for all the options in addition to the agreed simulation assumptions, if applicable<br>  - DRX configuration, including DRX periodicity, on duration, etc.<br>  - PDSCH is not scheduled in every slot<br>    - Provide details on the interval and arrive rate of PDSCH<br>      - E.g., PDSCH scheduled in burst of X slots, where X can be as low as 1.<br>  - Assumptions for PDSCH DMRS including<br>    - Precoding granularity: subband precoding, PRG size is reported by companies<br>    - DMRS<br>      - When DMRS is used for tracking, at least one additional DMRS symbol should be configured<br>      - The frequency domain location of DMRS may be changed across slots<br>      - The DMRS has a same bandwidth with PDSCH<br>  - SNR range: Low, media, high<br>  - Other aspects can be reported by companies | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Other Aspects related to CSI]` |
| Agreement | For the study and evaluation on the time domain factors for RS for finer time and frequency tracking<br>- Regarding the periodicity for periodic and semi-persistent RS, consider the following candidate values:<br>- Regarding the time interval  between RS symbols in a slot, consider the following candidate values:<br>- Regarding the number of symbols in a slot , consider the following candidate values:<br>- Regarding the number of consecutive slots , consider the following candidate values:<br>- The applicable FR should be considered. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Other Aspects related to CSI]` |
| Agreement | Regarding the minimal bandwidth () of RS spanning continuous RBs for finer time and frequency tracking, study and evaluate the following candidate values<br>- Full BWP bandwidth<br>- 52RBs<br>- 24RBs<br>- PDSCH bandwidth if DMRS is used for finer time and frequency tracking,<br>- SSB or other types of 6GR sync signals/channel structure or reference signal bandwidth (if applicable) when it is used for finer time and frequency tracking<br>- Other values are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Other Aspects related to CSI]` |
| Agreement | Study and evaluate the following assistance information for joint DL/UL based CSI acquisition use case 2<br>- SINR<br>- Non-PMI CSI feedback, e.g., CRI-RI-CQI<br>- RSRP + interference-plus-noise<br>- Note: The information can be wideband or subband, if applicable<br>- Note: How to calculate, use and report the information shall be reported by companies. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Other Aspects related to CSI]` |
| Agreement | Study and evaluate the following assistance information for joint DL/UL based CSI acquisition use case 1<br>- Channel covariance matrix related information<br>  - E.g., spatial/beam, frequency, or joint spatial frequency domain channel covariance matrix<br>- Multipath components (MPC) related information<br>  - E.g., Power Angular Spectrum (PAS), Power Delay Profile (PDP), Doppler shift/spread, Power Delay Angular and Doppler profile<br>- Channel matrix related information<br>  - E.g., Precoding matrix, channel eigenvector and eigenvalue, explicit channel matrix, etc.<br>  - Full or partial channel matrix related information may be used<br>- Received power difference between UE Rx ports<br>- L1-RSRPs with finer granularity in frequency (e.g., subband level) and/or spatial (e.g., Rx ports group)<br>Note 1: When the information is needed, how to report the information will be discussed in 10.5.3.1.<br>Note 2: How to calculate, use and report the information shall be reported by companies.<br>Note 3: The feasibility to obtain and report the information should be considered. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Other Aspects related to CSI]` |
| Agreement | For quantization of target CSI for NW side data collection, support amplitude and phase quantization with 3-bit for amplitude and 4-bit for phase for each complex number of the target CSI.<br>- Reuse eType II amplitude quantization table.<br>- Reuse eType II phase quantization table<br>- Support normalization Alt2-1:<br>- At least one element among the elements in the per-layer and per-subband target precoding matrix correspond to the strongest value in the amplitude quantization levels. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604677](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604677.zip) |
| Agreement | Decide the necessity of target CSI restriction in RAN1 #126 considering the following cases:<br>- Case A: No restriction on target CSI , NW further filter out the unwanted beams on the reconstructed CSI  such that , where  and<br>- Case B: Target CSI restriction is applied on  unwanted beams such that , NW further filter out the unwanted beams on the reconstructed CSI  such that , where  and<br>Metric: Investigate the resultant spectral efficiency or throughput of the two cases.<br>Note1: Models in both cases are trained without considering target CSI restriction as baseline. Companies to report if target CSI restriction is assumed in training for Case B.<br>Note2: Consider  as baseline (other values are not precluded) and different unwanted beams can be assumed for different samples (upto companies to report how to determine the unwanted beams for each sample)<br>Note3: non-oversampling beams are considered.<br>Note4: Companies to report UE side complexity analysis for both cases. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604677](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604677.zip) |
| Agreement | In NW data collection, UE reports target CSI according to the configured rank by the NW.<br>- FFS whether when the rank observed by UE is different from the configured rank, UE can report its suggested rank per reported target CSI sample<br>- FFS whether define data logging event<br>- FFS whether UE report data quality indicator and its type<br>- Note: Companies are encouraged to justify the usefulness of the above suggested rank, data logging event or data quality indicator via evaluations. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604678](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604678.zip) |

| Agreement | Study QCL framework, including QCL property, QCL relationship/chain between source QCL-RS(s) and target channel(s)/RS(s).<br>- Study the following QCL properties at least for DL as a starting point:<br>  - Doppler shift, Doppler spread, average delay, delay spread, average gain, spatial Rx parameter<br>  - Note: The above does not necessarily mean that the above is a part of QCL properties by default.<br>- Study source QCL-RS(s) w.r.t. QCL properties of each target channel/RS<br>  - Each target channel/RS can be associated with at least one source QCL-RS as a baseline<br>    - Study whether/how to introduce more than one source QCL-RS w.r.t. a same QCL property for a target channel/RS<br>    - Study whether/how to introduce more than one source QCL-RS w.r.t different QCL properties for a target channel/RS<br>- Study QCL relationship for multi-carrier/TRP/cell scenarios | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | For UE-initiated beam management in 6GR, study the following as an event evaluation metric as starting point.<br>-        measured or predicated L1-RSRP<br>-        L1-SINR<br>-        Hypothetical BLER.<br>For UE-initiated beam management in 6GR, study the following as report metric for beam reporting if applicable as starting point.<br>-        measured or predicated L1-RSRP<br>-        L1-SINR | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | On NW-initiated beam management, regarding DL beam measurement and report procedure on DL RS(s) for both single-TRP/cell/carrier and multi-TRP/cell/carrier scenario(s), the following aspects on DL beam measurement can be studied<br>- Study the procedure of beam sweeping considering the following alternatives:<br>  - At least support inter-symbol NW/UE-side beam sweeping including multiple DL RS(s) for DL Tx beam sweeping/repetition.<br>  - Whether/how to support the following on intra-symbol beam sweeping<br>    - Frequency-domain/FDMed at least for NW Tx beam sweeping: multiple DL RS(s) for beam measurement are multiplexed in/associated with different frequency resource(s)<br>    - Sequence-domain/CDMed at least for NW Tx beam sweeping: multiple DL RS(s) for beam measurement are multiplexed with different sequence (e.g., sequence root, or CDMed);<br>    - Sub-symbol/TDMed at least for UE Rx beam sweeping: each sub-symbol (e.g., time-domain multiplexing within a symbol) is measured by a respective Rx beam;<br>      - Note: ‘Sub-symbol’ is just for discussion purpose, which does not imply whether to introduce any new terminologies.<br>  - Note: UE-side beam sweeping is applied to the case of Rx multi-beam in FR2.<br>- Note: Granularity of DL RS can be further discussed.<br>- Study functionality requirement on DL RS measurement<br>  - Per DL measurement or across DL measurement(s), the following can be considered:<br>    - The maximum number of TRPs (within a cell or across cell)/cells/carriers<br>    - The maximum number of DL Tx beams per TRP/cell/carrier<br>    - The maximum number of DL Tx beams across measured TRPs/cells/carriers<br>- At least following aspects to be considered for designing the procedure of beam sweeping/tracking:<br>  - Increase of the number of coordinated TRPs/beams-per-TRP compared with 5G-NR<br>  - Hybrid/analog beam-forming architecture in NW<br>  - Latency/RS overhead for beam sweeping/tracking | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |

Others are not precluded (companies to report) \|<br>\| #16 Beam correspondence \| Ideal (baseline) or non-ideal. Others are not precluded. \|<br>\| #17 Link adaptation \| Based on CSI-RS (Adaptive Modulation and Coding, AMC) \|<br>\| #18 UE receiver type \| MMSE-IRC as baseline; other advanced receivers are not precluded \|<br>\| #19 BS Tx Power \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| #20 Maximum UE Tx Power \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| #21 Inter site distance \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions, besides for the following: High Speed Train: 1732 m ISD \|<br>\| #22 UE antenna element radiation pattern \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| #23 UE Speed and UE distribution \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions, besides for the following: High Speed Train: 100% outdoor in train (up to 500km/h) \|<br>\| #24 UE rotation \| No UE rotation (baseline). UE rotation is not precluded and the corresponding rotation speed is up to companies to report. \|<br>\| #25 BS Antenna height \| Dense urban macro and Urban Macro: 25 m; Dense urban micro: 10 m; Indoor: 3m \|<br>\| #26 UE Antenna height \| Section 7.2 in TR 38.901. Others are not precluded (companies to report). \|<br>\| #27 Car penetration Loss \| TR 38.901, sec 7.4.3.2: μ = 9 dB, σp = 5 dB \|<br>\| #28 Building penetration loss (O2I) \| Table 7.4.3-2 in TR 38.901 \|<br>\| #29 UE mobility feature (optional) \| Baseline: Random direction straight-line trajectories (from 38.843, Option-3) - Initial UE location, moving direction and speed: UE is randomly dropped in a cell, and an initial moving direction is randomly selected, with a fixed speed. - The initial UE location should be randomly drop within the following blue area: where d1 is the minimum distance that UE should be away from the BS. Each sector is a cell and that the cell association is geometry based. Company to report whether inter-cell handover or switching is disabled during the simulation. Company to report whether the trajectory should be terminated (e.g., single-TRP) or be retained (e.g., multi-TRP), if the UE trajectory hits the site boundary (the hexagonal grid) Other UE mobility model(s), e.g., EVM for NR ICBM, linear trajectory model with random and smooth direction change (i.e., TR38.843, Option-2), are not precluded (companies to report). \|<br>\| #30 Scheduling algorithm \| PF scheduler \|<br>\| #31 MCS \| Use NR MCS \|<br>\| #32 Traffic Model \| FTP model 1/3 with packet size 0.5 Mbytes (other value is not precluded) Other traffic models are not precluded (companies to report). Full buffer (Optional) \|<br>\| #33 CSI Feedback \| CSI periodicity: 10ms, 20ms (baseline), 40ms, 80ms, 160ms FFS: Other update for high-speed train \|<br>\| #34 Resource Utilization \| 30%, 50%, 70% (optional) \|<br>\| #35 Prediction parameter \| Company can report assumption for set-A and set-B, prediction window and any extension for intra-/inter-cell/multi-TRP measurement Set A: M*N M: {8, 16, 32, 64, 256,1024}. N: {1, 2, 3, 4}. Ratio of the size of Set-B to Set-A: {1, 1/2, 1/4, 1/8, 1/16, 1/32} For multi-TRP, Set-A and Set-B can contain beams across TRP(s). Other values for M, N and ratio are not precluded. Set A/Set B combinations: Alt1: Set B is different from Set A Alt2: Set B is a subset of Set A Alt3: Set B is the same as Set A Regarding temporal domain prediction, the following can be considered: Time gap between neighboring predicted time instances: {20ms, 40ms, 80ms, 120ms, 160ms, 200ms}. Predicted time instances: {1, 2, 4, 8}. Measured time instances: companies to report. Other values for the above are not precluded Other prediction mechanism is not precluded and up to companies’ report. \|<br>\| #36 UE number/cell \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| #37 Layout/deployment \| Reuse the assumptions of uplink/downlink transmission schemes (10.5.2.2/3) 7 x 3, single layer, hex grid (baseline) 7 x 3 two layers, macro layer: hex grid; micro layer: locations of micro sites are reported by company (optional) 19 x 3, single layer, hex grid (optional) FFS: Other update for high-speed train Regarding high-speed train, reuse the assumptions of downlink/uplink transmission schemes (10.5.2.2/3) Regarding around 4 and 7 GHz, rural macro, 500 m or 1732 m ISD between RRH sites, two TRxPs per RRH site, one or more RRH sites connected to a BBU, with RRH site to railway track distance: 100 m Inter-track distance: 6 m Train length: 400 m Train width: 3.38 m Regarding around 30 GHz, rural macro, 500 m or 1732 m between BBU sites, one or more RRH sites connected to 1 BBU, one TRxP per RRH site, with RRH site to railway track distance: 5 m Inter-RRH site distance: [580 m, 580 m, 572 m] Inter-track distance: 6 m Train length: 400 m Train width: 3.38 m \|<br>\| #38 Inter-cell interference model \| Reuse the assumptions of DL-based CSI (10.5.3.1) Explicitly and realistically modelled \|<br>\| #39 Metric \| Top-K/1 beam prediction accuracy (with or without L1-RSRP margin), average/5%-ile UPT/throughput, SINR/RSRP, L1-RSRP/SINR difference between Top-1 predicted beam and genie-aided beam, RS overhead reduction, beam management latency, and/or number of model-parameter(s)/model computational complexity. Companies can report other performance metrics Companies report the assumption of transmission scheme, if UPT/throughput is reported. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |

Others are not precluded (companies to report). \|<br>\| #10 Beam-forming scheme \| Companies explain the details, e.g., DFT-based or Eigen-based \|<br>\| #11 Procedure of beam sweeping \| Companies explain the details \|<br>\| #12 Criteria for beam selection \| To maximize RSRP (with/without a margin (baseline for margin: 3dB, companies to report)) as a baseline. | Agreement | For the study on DL beam measurement and report (AI/ML-based or non-AI/ML-based) on the deployment scenarios of NW multi-TRP/panel and/or UE single/multi-panel/irregular-handheld/CPE UE, the following can be considered:<br>- Potential use cases (multi-TRP transmission scheme may use the following beam measurement and report procedure):<br>  - Case-1: To identify/report the preferred N>1 DL beams per NW TRP/panel or across NW TRP(s)/panel(s) for supporting multi-TRP/panel transmission scheme(s).<br>  - Case-2: To identify/report the preferred M >1 DL beams of different NW TRPs/panels that can be received simultaneously to facilitate multi-TRP/panel joint transmission.<br>  - Other use cases are not precluded.<br>- For each potential use case, studying details of:<br>  - Benchmark:<br>    - Beam measurement and report (non-group based L1-RSRP report) on single-TRP/single-panel-UE, multi-TRP/single-panel-UE, of 5G NR<br>    - 5G NR L1-RSRP<br>  - L1-RSRP calculation<br>  - RS measurement requirements<br>  - Report content/format<br>  - Others are not precluded.<br>- Note-1: Strive for unified beam measurement and report procedure for all NW single-/multi-TRP/panel and single-panel-/multi-panel/irregular-handheld-UE.<br>- Note-2: Which multi-TRP transmission scheme to be supported will be discussed under other agenda.<br>- Companies report the assumption of transmission scheme, if UPT/throughput is reported. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | Regarding DL beam measurement and report procedure<br>(at least for non-AI/ML-based), for the study on L1-SINR based DL beam measurement and report, the following is considered:<br>- Studying details of<br>  - Benchmark: L1-RSRP based DL beam measurement and report<br>  - L1-SINR calculation<br>  - Resource requirements for channel and interference measurement<br>  - Report content<br>  - Others are not precluded.<br>- Potential design target/benefits/Use case(s):<br>  - Case-1: To reduce intra-TRP/cell interference<br>  - Case-2: To reduce cross-TRP/cell interference<br>  - Other cases | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | Support the following as clarification on urban grid as in the assumption of SLS for evaluation of 6GR beam management<br>- Inter site distance: 500 m ISD, 250 m ISD<br>- Layout/deployment:<br>  - 5 x 3 single layer, hex grid (baseline)<br>  - 18x 3 single layer, hex grid (baseline) | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | For the study on spatial/temporal/frequency domain beam measurement/prediction (relevant to AI/ML or non-AI/advanced schemes), the following is considered:<br>- Regarding frequency domain beam measurement/prediction (cross-frequency),<br>  - The following pair of measured/predicted frequencies can be considered for further evaluation.<br>    - Case-1:<br>      - Measured frequency: [4 GHz]<br>      - Predicted frequency: [7 GHz]<br>    - Case-2:<br>      - Measured frequency: [4 GHz]<br>      - Predicted frequency: [30 GHz]<br>    - Other cases are not precluded.<br>  - For the study of cross-frequency DL Tx beam prediction for collocated source and target frequencies (using same placement of NW antenna(s) for source and target frequencies), the following procedure for channel model generation can be considered as a starting point:<br>    - Step 1: To generate channel layout/properties for all TRPs (including both source and target) operating at the source frequency (e.g., 4GHz).<br>    - Step 2: To extend channels of target-frequency TRPs to target frequency (e.g., 7GHz) based on correlation modeling for multi‑frequency simulations (in TR 38.901)<br>    - Step 3: To remove the source-frequency (e.g., 4GHz) channels from these target prediction TRPs.<br>  - Note: RAN1 identify the requirement of channel modelling for evaluating<br>    - non-collocated source and target frequencies<br>    - collocated source and target frequencies (using [same] different height of NW antenna(s) and/or different orientation)<br>- Study AI/ML model generalization by evaluating inference performance of spatial/temporal/frequency domain beam measurement/prediction under different generalization cases.<br>  - Note: Identified generalization cases in 38.843 can be considered as a starting point | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |

| Agreement | For object detection and/or tracking, define velocity accuracy for 6G ISAC evaluation:<br>Velocity accuracy is defined as the absolute value of the difference between the estimated velocity and the corresponding true velocity of a sensing target. For single node monostatic sensing or single pair of bistatic sensing, both the radial velocity accuracy and the 3D velocity accuracy can be estimated. The true radial velocity is the sum of the following two components:<br>- Component 1: The projection of the velocity difference vector on the unit-norm vector from target to STX, where the velocity difference vector is between the true 3D velocity of the target and the true 3D velocity of the STX;<br>- Component 2: The projection of the velocity difference vector on the unit-norm vector from target to SRX, where the velocity difference vector is between the true 3D velocity of the target and the true 3D velocity of the SRX.<br>Note: If STx and SRx are static, true radial velocity is , where  is the true 3D velocity of the target,  is the bistatic angle between incident direction and scattering direction, and  is the angle between the true 3D velocity vector and the bisector vector within the bistatic angle . For mono-static sensing, . | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603926](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603926.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |

the channel of all Sensing Txs/Rxs Note: Selection of STx-SRx pair shall be based on achievable measurement information (e.g. RSRP, pathloss, RSRPP, etc.), but not genie-aided information of the target (e.g. location of the target, range between target and STx/SRx, etc.). \| Companies report the option and selection method for STx-SRx pair: Option 1: a same target is modelled in the channel
