| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G ISAC performance evaluation, adopt the following evaluation assumption table for baseline case/combination of sensing AGV.<br>\|  \| Parameter \| Sensing AGV \|<br>\| System configuration \| Scenario \| Indoor Factory (SH) \|<br>\| System configuration \| Carrier frequency and spectrum \| 4 GHz, TDD ~ 7 GHz, TDD 30 GHz, TDD \|<br>\| System configuration \| Waveform \| CP-OFDM can be used Other waveforms are up to companies report \|<br>\| System configuration \| Subcarrier spacing (when OFDM-based waveform is applied) \| 30 kHz for 4 GHz and ~ 7 GHz 120 kHz for 30 GHz \|<br>\| System configuration \| CP length (when OFDM-based waveform is applied) \| Normal CP as in communication can be used. Other values are up to companies report \|<br>\| System configuration \| Reference signal \| 6GR communication RS or existing 5G NR communication RS can be used as starting point. Other RS are up to company report \|<br>\| System configuration \| Information on sensing RS, assumed TDD UL/DL configuration if applicable \| Companies to report the necessary information of sensing RS, e.g. pattern in time and frequency domain, assumption in spatial domain, sequence, TDD configuration (if applicable), etc. \|<br>\| System configuration \| Bandwidth \| 100 MHz for 4 GHz 200 or 400 MHz for ~ 7 GHz Maximum 200 MHz for UE transmission 400 MHz for 30 GHz \|<br>\| System configuration \| Layout \| ISD = 50 m Follow the layout (InF-SH, sparse clutter, high BS) in TR 38.901/TR 38.914 \|<br>\| System configuration \| Wrap-round \| No wrap-round \|<br>\| BS configuration \| BS antenna height \| 8 m \|<br>\| BS configuration \| BS transmit power (per 20 MHz) \| For 4 GHz and ~7 GHz 24 dBm Note: BS Tx power scales up with bandwidth proportionally under the limitation of the maximum BS Tx power is 33 dBm for indoor for the above carrier frequencies. For 30 GHz 23 dBm Note: For around 30GHz, BS Tx power scales up with bandwidth proportionally under the limitation of EIRP 75dBm. Other BS transmit powers are not precluded (companies to report) Note: Tx power may be further limited due to mono-static sensing. \|<br>\| BS configuration \| BS antenna configuration \| 4 GHz: 32 TXRUs, 32 AEs, (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) = (4, 4, 2, 1 ,1; 4, 4), (dH, dV) = (0.5, 0.5) , (“Indoor Combination 1”) 32 TXRUs, 128 AEs, (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) = (8, 8, 2, 1 ,1; 2, 8), (dH, dV) = (0.5, 0.5) , (“Indoor Combination 2”) Around 7 GHz: 32 TxRU, 64 AEs, (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) = (4, 8, 2, 1, 1; 2, 8), (dH, dV) = (0.5, 0.5) , (“Indoor Combination 1”) 128 TxRU, 512 AEs, (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) = (16, 16, 2, 1, 1; 8, 8), (dH, dV) = (0.5, 0.5) , (“Indoor Combination 3”) 30 GHz 8 TxRU, 128 AEs, (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) = (4, 4, 2, 2, 2; 1, 1), (dH, dV) = (0.5, 0.5) , (“Indoor Combination 1”) 2 TxRU, 768 AEs, (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) = (24, 16, 2, 1, 1; 1, 1), (dH, dV) = (0.5, 0.5) , (“Indoor Combination 4”) Other antenna configurations are not precluded (companies to report) \|<br>\| BS configuration \| BS noise figure \| Around 7GHz and below: 5 dB Around 15GHz and above: 7 dB \|<br>\| BS configuration \| BS mechanical/electrical tilt \| Mechanical tilt: 180° in GCS Electrical tilt: Company to report, if other values than the mechanical tilt is used. \|<br>\| Sensing capable UE configuration \| UE antenna height \| Handheld: 1 m CPE: 1 m \|<br>\| Sensing capable UE configuration \| UE Transmit power (maximum) \| Handheld: 23 dBm as baseline For 30 GHz, EIRP should not exceed 43 dBm. CPE: 35 dBm as baseline For 30 GHz, EIRP should not exceed 55 dBm. Other values (e.g. 26 dBm, 29 dBm) are not precluded, but should follow those in communication evaluation assumption agenda. Note: Tx power may be further limited due to mono-static sensing. \|<br>\| Sensing capable UE configuration \| UE antenna configuration \| Handheld: 4 GHz and ~ 7GHz: Combination2(4R) Alt 1: (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) = (2, 2, 1, 1, 1; 2, 2) for single polarization, (dH,dV)= (0.5, 0.5)λ Alt 2: [(2, 4, 6, 8), or (1, 3, 5, 7)] as described in section 7.3 in TR 38.901 Combination3(8R) 30 GHz: Config 0: 1 panel，(M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) = (4, 1, 2, 1, 1; 1, 1) Config 1: 2 panels on front and back, 8 elements per panel (M, N, P) = (2, 2, 2), 2T2R TxRU per panel Other configurations are not precluded. Companies to report other details if necessary, e.g. device antenna model CPE: 4 GHz: Combination3(8R) Alt 1: (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) = (2, 2, 2, 1, 1; 2, 2) for dual polarization or (2, 4, 1, 1, 1; 2, 4) for single polarization , (dH, dV)= (0.5, 0.5)λ Alt 2: (1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8) as described in section 7.3 in TR 38.901 ~ 7 GHz: Combination4(16R) Alt 1: (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np)= (2, 4, 2, 1, 1; 2,4) , (dH, dV)= (0.5, 0.5)λ; Alt 2: up to company to report the antenna placement, directional pattern orientation of the CPE panel. 30 GHz: Config 0: (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) = (4, 4, 2, 1, 1; 1, 1); Config 1: 2 panels on front and back, 8 elements per panel (M, N, P) = (2, 2, 2), 2T2R TxRU per panel Other configurations are not precluded. CPE can be equipped with multiple (up to 4) antenna panels. Companies to report other details if necessary, e.g. device antenna model \|<br>\| Sensing capable UE configuration \| UE array orientation \| Handheld: Follow the models in communication evaluation assumption agenda CPE: Follow the models in communication evaluation assumption agenda Note: The orientation of the CPE panel can be optimized. \|<br>\| Sensing capable UE configuration \| UE mobility \| Handheld: Baseline: 0 km/h Optional: 3 km/h CPE: 0 km/h \|<br>\| Sensing capable UE configuration \| UE distribution \| Handheld: A number of N = {1, 10, 20 30, 60(baseline), 90(baseline), 120, 180} UEs uniformly distributed in the horizontal area of the convex hull. CPE: A number of N = {1, 10, 20, 30(baseline), 60, 90} UEs uniformly distributed in the horizontal area of the convex hull. Other values are not precluded. \|<br>\| Sensing capable UE configuration \| UE noise figure \| Around 7GHz and below: 7 dB 30 GHz: 10 dB \|<br>\| Sensing target/object configuration (AGV) \| Outdoor/indoor \| 100% Indoor \|<br>\| Sensing target/object configuration (AGV) \| Physical information and RCS \| Size (Length x Width x Height) Option 1: 0.5 m x 1.0 m x 0.5 m Option 2: 1.5 m x 3.0m x 1.5 m RCS based on Clause 7.9.2.1 in TR 38.901 Option 1: single scattering point \|<br>\| Sensing target/object configuration (AGV) \| 3D distribution \| N targets uniformly distributed over the horizontal area of the convex hull Baseline: N = 5 Optional: N = 1, 2, 10, 15 NOTE: N = 0 may be considered for the evaluation of false alarm \|<br>\| Sensing target/object configuration (AGV) \| Mobility \| Option 1: Uniform distribution in the range of up to 30 km/h Option 2: Fixed velocities {3, 10} km/h \|<br>\| Sensing target/object configuration (AGV) \| Orientation \| Random, horizontal plane only \|<br>\| Sensing target/object configuration (AGV) \| Trajectory \| Up to company report \|<br>\| Sensing target/object configuration (AGV) \| Minimum 3D distance between STx/SRx and sensing target \| Min distances based on min TRP/UE distances defined in TR 38.901 \|<br>\| Sensing target/object configuration (AGV) \| Minimum target-target 3D distance \| Option A: At least larger than the physical size of a target Option B: Fixed value, 5 m \|<br>\| Sensing target/object configuration (AGV) \| LOS/NLOS \| LOS and NLOS \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| Inter-site interference \| Option 1: Not modelled Option 2: Explicitly modelled Including interference from communication signal, if applicable. \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| Co-site inter-sector interference \| Not applicable \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| Concatenation of TX-target and target-RX links \| The NLOS rays in the STX-SPST link are 1-by-1 randomly coupled with the NLOS rays in the ST-SRX link. \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| The power threshold for path dropping after concatenation for target channel \| -40 dB for concatenation \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| CPI length \| Companies to report Note: separate discussion for waveform/RS \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| Periodicity of CPI for the same STx/SRx pair \| Companies to report \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| Number of CPI within one period for the same STx/SRx pair \| Companies to report \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| Overhead of sensing resource \| Both Overhead 1 and Overhead 2 ((defined in TR 38.765) shall be reported Overhead 1 (Type_1 + Type_3) over all radio DL and UL resources Overhead 2 (Type_1 – Type_2 + Type_3) over all radio DL and UL resources Overhead 2 less than 10% is encouraged \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| Selection of sensing Tx-Rx pair \| Companies report the option and selection method for STx-SRx pair: Option 1: a same target is modelled in the channel of single Sensing Tx/Rx Option 2: a same target is modelled in the channel of multiple Sensing TXs/RXs Option 3: a same target is modelled in the channel of all Sensing TXs/RXs Selection of STx-SRx pair shall be based on achievable measurement information (e.g. RSRP, pathloss, RSRPP, etc.), but not genie-aided information of the target (e.g. location of the target, range between target and STx/SRx, etc.). \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| Tx/Rx beam information, (mt x nt) / (mr x nr) \| Companies report the Tx and Rx beam information, of BS and/or UE, when applicable. \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| Algorithm for detection \| Companies report the algorithm for detection, including whether/how fusion of sensing data from multiple SRx is used \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| Algorithm for tracking \| Companies report the algorithm for tracking, if applicable, including whether/how fusion of sensing data from multiple SRx is used \|<br>\| Simulation assumption \| Other additional features \| Optional Up to companies report (e.g. low power clutter, clutter mobility). \|<br>FFS: AGV UE as sensing capable UE | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603928](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603928.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G ISAC evaluation on communication assistance, the following SAC (sensing assisted communication) evaluation scope is considered:<br>- Further discuss the sensing measurement/result used to assist communication, which may include, e.g.:<br>  - Delay/Doppler/angle/power/phase related information of the ISAC channel<br>  - Information of detected objects (static or moving) and/or large size surface (e.g. EO type-2)<br>- Further discuss evaluation of the performance of sensing measurement/results, which are used to assist communication.<br>  - Scenario: At least Urban Grid is considered<br>    - Urban Grid with or without EO type-2 can both be considered<br>    - Other scenarios are not precluded.<br>  - FFS detailed evaluation assumptions, sensing modes, prior information of EO-type2, etc.<br>  - When evaluated, company should report:<br>    - Performance metrics on sensing measurement/result used to assist communication<br>    - How such sensing measurement/result is able to assist the communication<br>    - The corresponding performance of sensing measurement/results<br>- Further discuss evaluation of communication performance assisted by sensing.<br>  - SAC use cases that observed in AI 10.8.2 can be considered<br>  - When evaluated, company should use the same scenario/assumptions (and ISAC channel if applicable) as sensing performance evaluation.<br>  - When evaluated, company should clarify whether ideal/non-ideal sensing measurement/result is used to assist communication.<br>  - When evaluated, company should report:<br>    - Communication-related performance metrics<br>    - Detailed methods on how the sensing measurements/results assist the communication<br>    - The corresponding communication performance gain/loss<br>Note: Strive for minimizing/no extension on existing channel model. It is up to company to use chapter 7/8 of TR38.901 for communication performance evaluation. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603928](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603928.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | - Study ISAC in all 6GR frequency ranges<br>- Study ISAC using each individual sensing mode<br>- Study ISAC using the combination of multiple sensing modes from RAN1 perspective | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603994](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603994.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - On time domain phase continuity for sensing RS, study at least the following aspects.<br>  - Definition of CPI<br>  - Factors that impact phase continuity in a CPI<br>  - Whether/how to maintain phase continuity in a CPI?<br>  - Whether/how to recover phase continuity in a CPI?<br>  - Impacts on sensing measurement/reporting<br>  - Other aspects are not precluded<br>- Companies are encouraged to bring up evaluation results including considered measurement and potential spec impacts on phase continuity issues in RAN1#126, e.g., the tolerable phase discontinuity. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603994](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603994.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - For study on resource multiplexing within sensing and between sensing and communication, consider at least the following aspects.<br>  - TDM/FDM/CDM/SDM/PDM (power domain multiplexing) multiplexing within sensing and between sensing and communication<br>  - Collison handling or signal sharing within sensing and between sensing and communication<br>  - Other aspects are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603994](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603994.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - RAN1 should identify at least one representative use case of sensing assisted communication for study in Rel-20<br>  - RAN1 to study the potential impacts if any on physical layer design for sensing assisted communication beyond what is necessary for object detection/tracking use-cases for UAV/human/Vehicle/AGV.<br>  - FFS: physical layer design aspects are limited to the scope of AI10.8 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603995](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603995.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - Study the necessity, scenario and related physical layer specification impacts of beam related aspects for RS used for sensing, taking communication beam management as starting point. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603996](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603996.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - Study the necessity, scenarios and related physical layer specification impacts of UE power control/TRP power allocation for RS used for sensing considering both sensing performance and impacts to communication | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603996](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603996.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - On TRP-side measurement report, measurement Level A from NR ISAC is not considered for 6G ISAC. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603996](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603996.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - Study the necessity, scenarios and related physical layer specification impacts of interference handling for sensing, to mitigate the interference within sensing and between sensing and communication, taking interference handling for communication as starting point. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603996](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603996.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - For study the necessity, scenarios and related physical layer specification impacts of sensing RS resource allocation, consider at least the following aspects.<br>  - Frequency resource allocation for sensing resource that may or may not be different from the frequency resource allocation for communication<br>  - Other aspects are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603996](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603996.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | Further study following options for evaluation of RE pattern of RS for sensing in addition to theoretical RE pattern characteristics analysis.<br>- Option 1<br>  - Full SLS as 10.8.1:<br>    - All evaluation assumptions/metrics/KPI agreed in 10.8.1 are reused<br>- Option 2<br>  - SLS with non-cooperative sensing (i.e., no fusion)<br>    - All evaluation assumptions/metrics agreed in 10.8.1 are reused except<br>      - LOS condition is always assumed for each STX-target-SRX link<br>      - FFS other simplification<br>- Option 3<br>  - SLS with single STX and single SRX<br>    - Evaluation assumptions/metrics agreed in 10.8.1 are reused except<br>      - LOS condition is always assumed for each STX-target-SRX link<br>      - FFS other simplification<br>- Option 4 (same way as 10.8.3)<br>  - Companies are encouraged to provide either LLS or a simplified SLS. It’s up to company to select and how to model LLS or a simplified SLS. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603997](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603997.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - Companies are encouraged to provide theoretical waveform/RS sequence characteristics analysis for waveform/RS sequence evaluation for 6G ISAC.<br>- Companies are encouraged to provide either LLS or a simplified SLS in addition to the theoretical waveform/RS sequence characteristics analysis. It’s up to company to select and how to model LLS or a simplified SLS. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604764](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604764.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Waveform and FS]` |
| Agreement | For the study of enhancement based on CP-OFDM for signal/RS used for sensing, at least the following methods can be considered.<br>- Zadoff-Chu sequence enhancement<br>- Gold sequence enhancement<br>- LFM/Chirp-like sequence (incl. frequency sweeping/Chirp rate/slope)<br>- Quasi-complementary sequence set (QCSS)<br>- Transparent/Non-transparent Tx/Rx time-domain processing and/or frequency-domain processing (e.g., FDSS)<br>- Pi/2 BPSK for RS<br>- Non-Linear FM sequence<br>- Pulse-type waveform/RS generation<br>- Phase continuity in the sensing resource block considering the guard band<br>- RS sequence randomization in time and/or frequency domain<br>- RS sequence spanning across multiple symbols<br>FFS: whether applicable to dedicated resource and/or shared resource with communication<br>FFS: The applicable sensing modes.<br>Note: Not all enhancements directions may be applicable for each of the above methods<br>Note: Other methods are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604764](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604764.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Waveform and FS]` |
| Agreement | For waveform/RS sequence evaluation for 6G ISAC, at least the following waveform/RS sequence characteristics are considered as the evaluation metrics.<br>- PAPR<br>- Tx power gain<br>  - PA model used should be reported by company<br>- Ambiguity function/auto-correlation property<br>  - PSLR / ISLR / main lobe width<br>  - FFS: details of the definitions, time/frequency/doppler domain, linear / circular correlation, etc.<br>- Cross-correlation<br>  - FFS: details of the definition<br>- Out-of-band characteristics<br>  - Adjacent Channel Leakage Ratio(ACLR) / Out-Of-Band Emission(OOBE)<br>  - PA model used should be reported by company<br>- Resilience to interference<br>  - Resilience to ISI, ICI, self-interference, inter-cell interference<br>  - FFS: details of metrics<br>FFS: whether applicable to dedicated resource and/or shared resource with communication<br>FFS: applicable metrics per evaluation scenario<br>Other metrics are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604764](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604764.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Waveform and FS]` |
| Agreement | For handling of a sensing signal reception beyond the communication CP length, if the necessity is identified, at least the following methods are studied for 6G ISAC without changing frame structure.<br>- Effective CP length extension based on successive N OFDM symbols<br>- Effective CP length extension based on frequency-domain comb pattern<br>- Time gap before/after/within sensing signal<br>- Effective CP length extension based on phase continuity across successive symbols<br>- RS sequence enhancement across symbols<br>- By Transmission of RS in successive N OFDM symbols without CP length extension implementation without any spec impact<br>FFS: applicable sensing modes<br>FFS: whether applicable to dedicated sensing RS or RS shared with communication<br>FFS: impact on integration with communication<br>FFS: resource overhead consideration<br>Note: Other methods are not precluded.<br>Note: RAN1 strives for a solution that has maximum commonality with 6GR communication frame structure and numerology. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604764](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604764.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-ISAC-Waveform and FS]` |

| Agreement | - Max rank for multi-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM:<br>  - Max rank = 2<br>- Codebook for two-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM:<br>  - For each precoder, each Tx port transmits at most one layer<br>  - Final decision is to be done in UL transmission agenda<br>- Relation of UE’s support of MaxRank for DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM in the uplink:<br>  - An UE supporting MaxRank = X (X=1 or 2) with DFT-s-OFDM supports at least the same MaxRank with CP-OFDM<br>- Waveform Switching<br>  - Support semi-static (RRC configured) PUSCH waveform determination for all RRC states operation<br>    - FFS: details<br>  - Support Dynamic PUSCH waveform determination for RRC connected mode operation<br>    - FFS: details<br>- Note: UE capability for above features can be discussed later once 6G UE capability framework is decided | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603725](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603725.zip) |
| Agreement | - FDSS with transparent filtering is beneficial to be supported for π/2-BPSK from RAN1 perspective<br>  - FFS: QPSK, 16QAM<br>- Draft LS to RAN4<br>  - FFS: contents e.g., “the exact filter requirements are up to RAN4 discussions.” | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603726](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603726.zip) |
| Agreement | Draft LS in R1-2605068 is endorsed in principle with following updates. Final LS in R1-2605069 is endorsed.<br>---<br>1	Overall description<br>RAN1 discussed coverage improvements through low PAPR enhancements for uplink DFT-s-OFDM waveform for 6GR.<br>RAN1#125 in May 2026 in Dalian reached an agreement that Frequency Domain Spectrum Shaping (FDSS) with transparent filtering is beneficial at least for π/2-BPSK. The exact wording of the RAN1 agreement is copied below:<br>\| Agreement: FDSS with transparent filtering is beneficial to be supported for π/2-BPSK from RAN1 perspective FFS: QPSK, 16QAM Draft LS to RAN4 FFS: contents e.g., “the exact filter requirements are up to RAN4 discussions.” \|<br>RAN1 has also been studying other PAPR enhancement techniques, with observations made in RA1#125.<br>2	Actions<br>To RAN4<br>ACTION: 	RAN1 respectfully asks RAN4 to take the RAN1 agreement on FDSS with transparent filtering into account in their work on 6GR.<br>3	Dates of next TSG RAN meetings<br>RAN WG#126		24th – 28th August, 2026	Maastricht, Netherlands<br>RAN WG#126bis	12th – 16th October, 2026	Jeju, Korea<br>--- | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605068](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605068.zip) |
| Agreement | - Followings are used as comparison baseline for further study<br>  - Net gain compared with legacy pi/2 BPSK with Rel-15 5G NR transparent FDSS and legacy QPSK/16QAM without FDSS<br>  - target spectral efficiency points provided by Rel-15 5G NR MCS table for UL DFT-s-OFDM waveform<br>- Companies are encouraged to report PA model used in the evaluation and whether transparent CFR is applied to the baseline<br>- Note: when enhancement technique applies transparent FDSS with ST, companies are encouraged to report at least the result using same transparent FDSS without ST when same modulation is used<br>- FFS: Support for FDSS-SE and corresponding spectral efficiency range<br>- FFS: Support for FDSS-ST and corresponding spectral efficiency range<br>- FFS: transparent/non-transparent filtering | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605068](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605068.zip) |
| Agreement | Following RAN1 report contents on waveform for communication to RAN are endorsed.<br>Waveform for communication<br>\| What RAN1 has discussed/observed/ agreed \| RAN1 agreed to support CP-OFDM for DL and CP-OFDM/DFT-s-OFDM for UL as defined in 5G NR for 6GR. RAN1 has prioritized the studies on “UL coverage improvement through low UL PAPR enhancement for DFT-s-OFDM” and “multi-rank UL-MIMO for DFT-s-OFDM”. RAN1 agreed to support maximum rank = 2 for multi-rank UL-MIMO for DFT-s-OFDM. RAN1 agreed to support semi-static and dynamic UL waveform switching RAN1 agreed for low UL PAPR enhancement for DFT-s-OFDM that for pi/2 BPSK, Frequency Domain Spectrum Shaping (FDSS) with transparent filtering is beneficial to be supported, and liaised RAN4 accordingly. RAN1 has captured interim observations on other PAPR enhancement techniques for UL DFT-s-OFDM RAN1 noted the study on other waveforms, and RAN1 concluded that further discussion is not planned in waveform agenda item in RAN1 unless tasked by RAN. \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans to continue studying after June \| On low UL PAPR enhancement for DFT-s-OFDM: Further work after June 2026 is required in order to draw definite conclusions on the benefits and potential RAN1 spec impacts of spectral extension/truncation techniques with transparent/non-transparent filtering, the benefits and potential RAN1 spec impacts of non-transparent filtering with no spectral extension/truncation techniques. \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans not to pursue after June \| RAN1 plans not to pursue the study on other waveforms than CP-OFDM for DL and CP-OFDM/DFT-s-OFDM for UL in waveform agenda item in RAN1 unless tasked by RAN. \|<br>\| What RAN1 requests RAN guidance/ decision if any \|  \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603728](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603728.zip) |
| Agreement | For the study of the new BG(s) and its corresponding PCM(s) for LDPC extension beyond NR range,<br>- Assume option B1 as agreed in RAN1#124bis for sub-matrix B, i.e.,<br>    - A weight-3 column with corresponding shifting values of [0, X, 0] or [X, 0, X], followed by a dual-diagonal structure with corresponding shifting values of [0,0]<br>- Assume option 1 agreed in RAN1#124bis with modification as below:<br>    - Row splitting, wherein one or more rows outside the core matrix (i.e., sub matrix A and sub matrix B) are split from at least some rows inside the core matrix, and the core column indices of the non-zero entries of the original row are a superset of those of the corresponding split row,<br>    - The core matrix contains Y1 rows, and all elements of Y2 rows in sub-matrix A are ones.<br>        - Y1= 3 or 4<br>        - Y1Y21<br>            - With at least the first row in sub-matrix A included in the Y2 rows<br>    - Quasi-row orthogonality in sub-matrix D, i.e., in which except for certain columns, the row 2k, 2k+1 do not share non-zero entries in the same column in the sub-matrix D<br>    - No puncturing before rate matching as starting point.<br>- Note: The design of new BG considers performance and computational complexity benefits for all applicable modulation order (e.g. 256 QAM and above) and code rates no less than approximately 2/3 at iterations (5, 7,10, 15) compared to BG1 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605016](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605016.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |

| Conclusion | Report the following for interim RAN1 report on Coding check point.<br>\| What RAN1 has discussed/observed/agreed \| RAN1 agreed for 6G channel coding, LDPC is used for data (including SIBs) and Polar code is used for L1 control information (larger than 11 bits, including PBCH). RAN1 agreed for 6G data channel coding: 1) working assumption is that reuse of NR LDPC design is supported for data rate within NR range; 2) study LDPC extension for data rate beyond NR range. In addition, RAN1 studied and identified the structure and parameters of potential LDPC extension. The evaluation results of LDPC code extension beyond NR range have been captured in R1-2605123. RAN1 agreed on the framework of application condition of potential LDPC extension, if introduced. RAN1 agreed for 6G control channel coding: 1) RAN1 working assumption is that for control information within NR range (larger than 11 bits), reuse of NR Polar code design is supported. RAN1 concluded there is no consensus on motivation(s) for potential extension/enhancement for Polar code design for payload size within NR range (larger than 11bits). 2) study Polar code extension for payload size beyond NR range based on the evaluation assumptions in RAN1#124. In addition, RAN1 agreed to study more than 2 segments for Polar code with UCI payload size larger than 1706 bits. RAN1 agreed for 6G small UCI with 3~11 bits: 1) RAN1 agreed to at least consider 5G RM code for small UCI with payload size of 3~11 bits, identify the justifiable drawback of 5G RM code, if exists, study potential solution(s). \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans to continue studying after June \| RAN1 plans to continue to study Polar code extension for payload size beyond NR range (larger than 11bits). RAN1 plans to continue to identify the justifiable drawbacks of 5G RM code, if exists, study potential solution(s). \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans not to pursue after June \| RAN1 plans not to pursue Polar code extension for payload size within NR range (larger than 11bits) after June. \|<br>\| What RAN1 requests RAN guidance/decision if any \| RAN1 needs RAN guidance on if and how to proceed on the remaining study for LDPC code extension. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605018](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605018.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| Agreement | - Regarding the proposed geometric shaping or probabilistic shaping for modulation enhancements for 6GR:<br>  - If any enhancements for modulation based on geometric shaping or probabilistic shaping for 6GR is supported, it can be configured by network in addition to equal probability uniform QAM that is already supported for 6GR.<br>  - With respect to enhancements for modulation based on geometric shaping or probabilistic shaping, the status is captured under A, B, C as the following:<br>- A) Regarding geometric shaping for 6GR, the following options have been considered:<br>    - Option 1: 1D-NUC<br>    - Option 2: 2D-NUC<br>      - Option 2-A: QAM-CS based 2D-NUC where the constellation points are selected from a larger uniform QAM constellation<br>      - Option 2-B: NP-NUC where the receiver assumes the constellation is uniform QAM<br>      - Option 2-C: Unconstrained constellation 2D-NUC<br>  - For further study of GS, if any, the following option is considered:<br>    - Option 1. 1D-NUC for modulation order Qm in the range of: Qm>=4<br>      - For quantization bit width Y per I/Q: Y<=[10] bits<br>      - Bit to symbol mapping for 1D-NUC: Gray mapping as in uniform QAM<br>  - Total number of NUC constellations is no larger than X for one MCS table<br>    - FFS: Value of X<br>  - If GS is further studied is a separate discussion<br>  - B) Regarding probabilistic shaping for 6GR, the following options have been considered:<br>    - Option1: ESS for PS DM<br>      - Option 1-A: No limitation on the number of shaped bits per I/Q symbol<br>      - Option 1-B: The number of shaped bits for each I or Q sample is no more than 2 bits<br>    - Option2: ECC-DM for PS DM, where the number of shaped bits for each I or Q sample is no more than 2 bits<br>    - Option3: CCDM for PS DM<br>      - Option 3-A: No limitation on the number of shaped bits per I/Q symbol<br>      - Option 3-B: The number of shaped bits for each I or Q sample is no more than 2 bits<br>    - Option4: Huffman coding-based shaping for PS DM<br>  - For further study of PS, if any, below options can be considered:<br>    - Option 1-B<br>    - Option 2<br>  - If PS is further studied is a separate discussion<br>  - For further study of PS schemes for 6GR, consider maximum DM/DDM block length of X I or Q samples<br>    - 64<=X<=256 for ESS based DM<br>    - X=1024 for ECC based DM<br>  - C) Regarding the proposed enhancements for modulation based on geometric shaping and probabilistic shaping for 6GR, none of the enhancements are needed. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605122](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605122.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| Conclusion | For interim RAN1 report on Modulation check point, the following changes are applied to R1-2605145:<br>  - For UL 1K QAM for FWA use cases, UPT performance is captured for some UL FWA UE in SLS<br>  - Enhancement of MCS table design<br>The updated interim RAN1 report on Modulation check point as following<br>\| What RAN1 has discussed/observed/agreed \| Agreed RAN1 agreed to at least support 5G NR equal probability uniform QAM constellation for 6G PS/GS: For PS, if any, down-selected to ECC-DM and ESS based DM with up to 2 shaped bits per I/Q symbol. For GS, if any, down-selected to 1D-NUC. Discussed For DL 4K QAM and UL 1K QAM, RAN1 discussed use cases, and defined SLS evaluation methodology. Multiple sources provided LLS and SLS evaluations. For constellation shaping, both GS and PS are being considered, with different schemes and parameter choices for each design. The complexity and spec impact for both GS and PS are evaluated and compared. Observed For constellation shaping, the fixed SE performance is evaluated across different channels and corresponding observations captured For constellation shaping, the complexity is evaluated and corresponding observations captured For UL 1K QAM for FWA use cases, UPT performance is captured for some UL FWA UE in SLS \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans to continue studying after June \| Enhancement of MCS table design \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans not to pursue after June \| PS/GS: PS other than ECC-DM and ESS based DM with up to 2 shaped bits per I/Q symbol. GS other than 1D-NUC \|<br>\| What RAN1 requests RAN guidance/decision if any \| RAN1 needs RAN guidance on if and how to proceed on the remaining study for UL 1K QAM, DL 4K QAM, and constellation shaping based enhancements for modulation \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605122](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605122.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

| Agreement | For aNB semi-static SBFD, study at least the following aspects<br>- SBFD time-frequency resource determination<br>- Random access procedure<br>- UE transmission, reception and measurement behaviors and procedures within non-SBFD/SBFD symbols, or across SBFD and non-SBFD symbols, including link direction determination in symbols with SBFD operation<br>  - Note: “non-SBFD symbols” and “SBFD symbols” are only for discussion purpose. Whether to introduce configuration of SBFD symbol type will be separately discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | For the RAN1 study of “Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band”,<br>- -999 dBm/Hz is used for (15) Receiver interference density<br>  - Note: Zero interference margin is assumed here for coverage gap identification<br>- Companies can additionally use the value(s) of (15) Receiver interference density based on SLS<br>  - Note: Non-zero interference margin is assumed here for coverage gap identification.<br>- RAN1 to discuss and agree on the value(s) for (15) from July 27th - August 7th 2026  and use it for coverage gap identification<br>- The identified coverage gap assuming zero interference margin and non-zero interference margin will be captured separately<br>- RAN1 to draw conclusions on the identified coverage gap assuming the non-zero interference margin(s)<br>Note: Interference margin (X dB) is defined as entry (16) - (14) - (13) in the link budget table<br>- (13) Receiver noise figure<br>- (14) Thermal noise density;<br>- (15) Receiver interference density: 10log(10^((X + (13) + (14))/10) - 10^(( (13) + (14))/10))<br>- (16) Total noise plus interference density: 10 log (10^(( (13) + (14))/10) + 10^((15)/10)) | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | Study the scenarios and use cases of multiple carriers where for initial access a UE can utilize resources for UL (Msg1, etc) or for both UL (Msg1, etc.) and DL (Msg2, etc.) in one carrier other than the carrier where the UE obtains the system information providing configuration for those resources.<br>- Study which resources can be provided by aNB<br>- Study how UE selects resources, including how UE obtains DL synchronization for the transmission of Msg1 including co-located and non-collocated deployment scenarios<br>- RAN1 to coordinate with RAN4 as necessary, e.g., timing, UL power control and any association between carriers | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | From RAN1 perspective, to support 6G-6G spectrum aggregation for non-collocated deployment scenarios for FR1-FR2 combination, assuming backhaul latency up to 10ms<br>- Both 6G-6G-DC with two cell groups and 6G-6G-CA with two PUCCH groups using separate TA per group to can support non-collocated FR1-FR2 spectrum aggregation with imperfect backhaul.<br>  - The physical layer functionalities for both solutions are similar.<br>- For 6G-6G-CA with a single PUCCH group, the following have been identified<br>  - Compared to 6G-6G-DC and 6G-6G-CA with two PUCCH groups, 6G-6G-CA with single PUCCH group may provide better UL coverage in the case the HARQ-ACK for FR2 is transmitted in FR1.<br>  - The larger HARQ RTT caused by the backhaul delay and rescheduling timeline can lead to HARQ process starvation and degrade the DL throughput.<br>- Note: the term of PUCCH group similar to 5G design is assumed here only to facilitate discussions and it does not imply anything agreed for 6G design. Similarly, the same clarification is applied to any 5G term used above. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | For the 6GR smallest maximum UE bandwidth for at least one lower-tier device for FDD with 15kHz SCS:<br>- Reasons for possible recommendation of 20MHz RF and BB bandwidth for both UL and DL (i.e. Alt 1) include:<br>  - If initial access is independent of UE’s bandwidth capability, which requires initial access for both DL and UL to be contained within 20MHz and 5 MHz for Alt 1 and Alt 2 respectively even for FDD bands with spectrum allocation larger than 5MHz, Alt 1 allows both the lowest-tier device and MBB devices to operate in the same band with better system performance in terms of DL coverage assuming same PSD and capacity for initial access, paging and random access compared with Alt 2.<br>    - For Alt 2, it results in lower performance for initial access for 6G MBB UEs compared to 5G MBB UEs, since 5G networks can configure a bandwidth larger than 5 MHz for initial access. But such DL coverage losses might be mitigated by techniques such as power boosting.<br>  - The additional UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 2 compared with Alt 1 is no more than 10% when the same number of duplexers/SAW-filters/switches is considered for all alternatives<br>  - In case of a multi-RAT device, UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 2 compared with Alt 1 may not be exploitable in all implementations, e.g. when the other RAT requires supporting a 20 MHz bandwidth.<br>  - Alt. 1 can reach the minimum PHY peak data rate within a larger DL coverage assuming same PSD by using a lower MCS in 20 MHz CBW compared with Alt. 2 but in that case a bandwidth larger than 5MHz has to be used.<br>- Reasons for possible recommendation of 5MHz RF and BB bandwidth for both UL and DL (i.e. Alt 2)  include:<br>  - Alt 2 achieves ~8-10% additional UE modem complexity reduction compared with Alt 1 for a device supporting single FDD band<br>  - 5MHz BW is feasible for all device types for initial access, paging and random access for FDD based on previous study and deployment experience<br>    - More scheduling flexibility is offered by Alt 1 compared with Alt 2 if initial access is common to all device types, when the available bandwidth is larger than 5 MHz in an FDD band if an operator wants to operate both lowest-tier and MBB UEs in the same band. UL scheduling flexibility loss can be partially mitigated if fast frequency retuning (1-2 symbols) can be supported by lowest-tier UEs.<br>- Reasons for possible recommendation of 20MHz RF bandwidth for both UL and DL, and 20MHz BB bandwidth for DL and 5MHz BB bandwidth for UL (i.e. Alt 3 with only BB BW reduction for UL) include:<br>  - If initial access is independent of UE’s bandwidth capability, which requires initial access for both DL and UL to be contained within 20MHz and 5 MHz for Alt 3 and Alt 2 respectively and UL transmission is limited to 5MHz within each symbol for Alt 3 even for FDD bands with spectrum allocation larger than 5MHz, Alt 3 allows both the lowest-tier device and MBB devices to operate in the same band with better system performance in terms of DL coverage assuming same PSD and capacity for initial access, paging and random access compared with Alt 2.<br>    - For Alt 2, it results in lower performance for initial access for 6G MBB UEs compared to 5G MBB UEs, since 5G networks can configure a bandwidth larger than 5 MHz for initial access. But such DL coverage losses might be mitigated by techniques such as power boosting.<br>  - The additional UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 2 compared with Alt 3 (with only BB BW reduction for UL) is no more than 7% for a device supporting single FDD band<br>  - The additional UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 3 (with only BB BW reduction for UL) compared with Alt 1 is less than 4% for a device supporting single FDD band.<br>  - Alt. 3 can reach the minimum PHY peak data rate within a larger DL coverage assuming same PSD by using a lower MCS in 20 MHz CBW compared with Alt. 2 but in that case a bandwidth larger than 5MHz has to be used.<br>- For all the three alternatives,<br>  - There are more 5MHz spectrums than 20MHz spectrums in sub-1 GHz bands available for lowest-tier devices, although some operators may only have spectrums larger than 5MHz for lowest-tier devices<br>  - For a device supporting multiple FDD bands, equipped with multiple band-specific analogue filters (duplex filters for FD-FDD, SAW filters for HD-FDD), when comparing on the basis of same PHY peak rate (~10 Mbps) and same number of band-specific analogue filters, the additional UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 2 and Alt 3 compared to Alt 1 is less than for single band device.<br>  - For HD-FDD UL, five sources in RAN1 [R1-2603776][R1-2603941][R1-2604259][R1-2604548] [R1-2605059] observe that elimination of band-specific SAW filters can provide a substantial (~ 30-60%) modem complexity reduction for multi-band devices.<br>    - RAN1 understands that multiple options are under discussion in RAN4 to support SAW-less UE implementation.<br>    - The feasibility and benefits of different RAN4 options to facilitate supporting SAW-less UE implementation for different RAN1 smallest maximum UE bandwidth alternatives is up to RAN4.<br>    - The outcome of the RAN4 study may have implications for the RF and/or BB bandwidth for HD-FDD UL. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | For the 6GR smallest maximum UE bandwidth for at least one lower-tier device, RAN1 recommends Alt 1 for TDD with 30kHz SCS<br>- Alt 1: 20MHz RF and BB bandwidth for both UL and DL<br>Note：No decision in RAN1 about the spectrum applicability and applicable frequency bands for lower-tier device.<br>Note:  Above agreement has no linkage to FDD | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | Update agreement for the study of link direction determination for dynamic TDD in RAN1#124bis as follows.<br>For the study of link direction determination for dynamic TDD, consider the following options:<br>- Option 1:<br>  - TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and flexible symbols<br>  - TDD pattern is semi-statically configured<br>  - No dynamic slot format indication as in NR<br>  - For dynamically scheduled single transmission/reception in flexible symbols, UE link direction is determined only based on scheduling DCI<br>  - Study transmissions/receptions without associated DCI other than the configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols (if configured)<br>  - For configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols, consider the following alternatives<br>    - Alt 1: Configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols are valid and the link direction of the flexible symbols are not expected to be overridden by DCI<br>      - FFS exceptions<br>    - Alt 2: Configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols are invalid<br>      - FFS exceptions<br>    - Alt 3: Configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols are valid and can be overridden by dynamic scheduled transmission/reception in the same symbol if sufficient processing time is provided<br>      - FFS exceptions<br>    - Other alternatives are not precluded<br>  - FFS: Maximum one DL-UL switching point within a TDD pattern<br>- Option 2:<br>  - Semi-statically configured TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and flexible symbols<br>  - Dynamically indicated TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and guard symbols<br>    - Dynamically indicated TDD pattern indicates DL, UL or guard symbols for flexible symbols according to semi-statically configured TDD pattern<br>    - The TDD pattern should be provided to the UE with sufficient processing time to apply the TDD pattern change<br>  - UE link direction is determined based on TDD pattern<br>  - Link direction is not expected to be changed within certain time window<br>  - Configured transmission/receptions are allowed on resolved flexible symbols<br>    - Resolved flexible symbols are the symbols that already been scheduled with DL or UL within the time window<br>  - FFS: Maximum one DL-UL switching point within a TDD pattern<br>- Option 3:<br>  - TDD pattern, based on DL, UL and guard symbols (i.e. no flexible symbols)<br>  - UE is dynamically indicated with a TDD pattern out of predefined/preconfigured TDD pattern<br>    - The TDD pattern should be provided to the UE with sufficient processing time to apply the TDD pattern change<br>- Option 4:<br>  - TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and flexible symbols<br>  - TDD pattern is semi-statically configured<br>  - No dynamic slot format indication as in NR<br>  - For dynamically scheduled single transmission/reception in flexible symbols, UE link direction is determined only based on scheduling DCI<br>  - Study transmissions/receptions without associated DCI other than the configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols (if configured)<br>  - Configured transmissions/receptions are valid only in resolved flexible symbols<br>    - Link direction is not expected to be changed within certain time window<br>    - Resolved flexible symbols are the symbols that already been scheduled with DL or UL within the time window<br>- Other options are not precluded<br>FFS: scenarios, where whether/how to mitigate CLI of adjacent frequency between inter-operator for dynamic TDD | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | For support of UEs operating with Maximum CBW of 400MHz in DL (i.e., non-CA single CC) and Maximum CBW of 200MHz in UL in a BS with a maximum CBW of 400MHz for around 7GHz, the frequency location of UL with a maximum CBW of 200MHz should be configured with certain flexibility within the entire configured DL bandwidth.<br>- Center frequencies of DL and UL can be different from UE perspective<br>- FFS configuration flexibility | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | The following template are used to capture the identified coverage gap between ~7GHz and 5G mid-band for different signals and channels during initial access,<br>FFS: where coverage gap is determined as the trimmed mean of all samples, trimmed mean is calculated by discarding the single lowest and highest values. or where coverage gap is determined as the median of all samples, and between these two, down-selection should be done before July 31<br>Table X: Coverage gap between 7.0 GHz and 5G mid-band (3.5 GHz) for different signals and channels during initial access [Template]<br>\| Signals/channels \| Number of Sample(s) \| Coverage gap (FFS: trimmed mean/median) \| Standard Deviation (FFS: trimmed/median) \|<br>\| PSS/SSS \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| PBCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| SIB1 PDCCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| SIB1 PDSCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| Msg2 PDSCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| Msg4 PDSCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| PRACH format 0 \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| PRACH format B4 \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| PRACH format C2 \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| Msg3 PUSCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| Msg5 PUSCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| PUCCH format 1 \|  \|  \|  \|<br>Single pair of reference carrier frequencies (i.e. 3.5 GHz and 7.0 GHz) is used to generate the table above. For the coverage results that have been reported by companies for 2.6 GHz carrier, a common gap between 2.6 GHz and 3.5 GHz for all signals/channels is captured as an observation by trimmed mean, or median of the samples of coverage gaps between Msg3@2.6 GHz and Msg3@3.5 GHz. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Conclusion | For the RAN1 study of “Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band”, to align the usage of the correction factors of BS antenna gain of common beams in co-site deployment evaluation, the following are observed as the common understanding<br>- the total antenna gain of BS DL common beam is assumed to be unchanged/comparable if the beamwidth of SSB beams is unchanged, regardless of whether the number of BS antenna elements or BS TxRUs increases or not.<br>  - For DL: The total BS antenna gain above refers to the sum value of entry (4)+(5)) in the link budget table.<br>- The total antenna gain of BS UL common beam is expected not to be smaller than that of BS DL common beam if the beamwidth of SSB beams is unchanged.<br>  - For UL: The total BS antenna gain above refers to the sum value of entry (11)+(11bis) in the link budget table.<br>Note:<br>- (4) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of transmitter = (4a) – (4b) (dB)<br>- (5) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of transmitter = (5a) - (5b) (dB) Note: zero for uplink<br>- (11) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of receiver = (11a) - (11b) (dB)<br>- (11bis) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of receiver = (11bis-a) - (11bis-b) (dB) Note: zero for downlink | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

| Agreement | Design of the common signals/channels (at least for SSB) for initial access by assuming bandwidth larger than 3MHz with 15kHz SCS, which is applicable to any spectrum allocations with adjustment, if applicable<br>Note: at least bandwidth of SSB larger than 5G NR should be well justified. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| Agreement | For idle mode mobility,<br>- At least 6GR SSS of 6GR SSB is supported as measurement resource.<br>  - FFS other signal in addition (e.g. other types of 6GR sync signal or reference signal, DMRS of PBCH)<br>- For measurement metric, at least RSRP is supported, FFS<br>  - RSRQ and the default SS-RSRQ definition if supported,<br>  - SINR,<br>and select one of those two in addition to RSRP | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| Agreement | Study the following options for 6GR SSB time-domain structure<br>- Option 1: one 6GR SSB spans X (4<=X<=7) symbols within a slot<br>  - Option 1-1:  one 6GR SSB spans 4 symbols within a slot<br>  - Option 1-2:  one 6GR SSB spans X (4<X <=7) symbols within a slot<br>- Option 2: one 6GR SSB spans Y (7<Y<=12) symbols within a slot | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| Agreement | For the purpose of discussing 6GR SSB burst pattern and associated default SSB periodicity, study the following:<br>- The max number of candidate 6GR SSBs in a 6GR SSB burst: X<br>- The max number of candidate 6GR SSB burst(s) in a default 6GR SSB periodicity: Y<br>- The max number of candidate 6GR SSB repetitions within a default 6GR SSB periodicity: M<br>- Default SSB periodicity for initial cell selection: T<br>- The max number of default 6GR SSB periodicities (similar as what is assumed for evaluation in 5G NR performance) for UE performing 6GR SSB combining: N<br>- Note: As an example, for NR in 3.5 GHz, X = 8, Y = 1, M = 1, N = 4, T = 20ms.<br>For the study on the values of X, Y, Nand M for a given default SSB periodicity T, at least SSB detection performance, UE complexity, UE measurement aspects should be considered. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| Agreement | For the study of 6GR SSB performance, the following can be assumed<br>- Alt.1: Combining is performed across all 6GR SSB repetitions within a 6GR SSB burst in case of intra-burst repetition within an SSB periodicity<br>- Alt.2: Combining is performed across all 6GR SSB repetitions across 6GR SSB bursts in case of inter-burst repetition (Y>1) within a SSB periodicity<br>- Alt.3: Combining is performed across all 6GR SSBs within a 6GR SSB burst<br>- Alt.4: Combining is performed across different SSB periodicities<br>- Alt.5: Combining is not performed across 6GR SSBs within a SSB periodicity when M = 1<br>- Alt.6: Combination of above alternatives<br>Note: To compare different 6GR SSB burst patterns, the same total number of SSBs (X*Y*N) within N*T are assumed.<br>Note: The above assumptions are only for evaluation purpose and do not mandate UE implementations.<br>Note: Companies to report which combining(s) is/are assumed in the study.<br>Note: Companies to report the combining details and assumptions<br>Note: Companies to report the burst pattern design details, e.g. the duration of the SSB burst, interval between different SSB burst.<br>Note: SSB performance, UE complexity, UE measurement aspects should be considered.<br>Note: Companies to report other related assumptions, e.g., whether power boosting is applied. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| Agreement | As a baseline, the number of 6GR physical cell IDs is 1008<br>- FFS: Motivation to extend or reduce the number of PCI | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| Agreement | 6GR synchronization signals (PSS and SSS) in 6GR SSB carry full PCI information as baseline<br>- FFS: Motivation to carry other information<br>- FFS: Motivation to carry part of PCI information by other signals, e.g., PBCH DMRS, if 6GR synchronization signals carry part of PCI information | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| Agreement | For initial access procedures starting from initial cell search and cell search of reselection till the completion of random access in multi-TRP based deployments, study the following options<br>- Option 1: Multiple TRPs are transparent to the UE, and corresponding definition about what transparent Multiple TRPs mean<br>- Option 2: Multiple TRPs are non-transparent to the UE, and corresponding definition about what non-transparent Multiple TRPs mean<br>Note: Companies are encouraged to study the coverage performance, network energy saving, the associated signalling overhead, access latency, UE complexity, and compatibility with single-TRP deployments for both options.<br>Note: RACH related procedure/behaviour will be discussed under AI 10.5.1.2 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| Agreement | Study the need, justification, and impact of increasing the maximum number of preambles per RO above 64 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |

| Agreement | Update the TRP antenna configuration for 4GHz from (8, 2, 2, 1, 1; 1, 2) (optional combination 0) to (12, 8, 2, 1, 1; 4, 8) (combination 1).<br>Remove brackets from PUSCH duration: [28] OS for the evaluation of TBoMS. (For agreement made for LLS for PUSCH in RAN#124b) | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | Update the UT antenna configuration as follows:<br>\| Antenna Configuration at the UE \| For TDL: Around 700 MHz, 2 GHz: 1T1R, 1T2R, 2T2R Around 2/4 GHz: 1T2R, 2T4R Around 7/15 GHz: 1T4R, 2T4R Around 30 GHz: 2T2R per panel as agreed in AI 10.1 For CDL: - Around 700 MHz, 2 GHz: 1T1R (Combination 0) - 700MHz, 2 GHz, 4 GHz: handheld UT model (from Clause 7.3.2 of TR38.901) with 1T2R (Combination 1), 1T4R (Combination 2), 2T4R (Combination 2) - 7 GHz, 15 GHz: handheld UT model (from Clause 7.3.2 of TR38.901) with 1T4R, 2T4R (Combination 2) - 30GHz (as agreed in AI 10.1): Company to report which UE antenna configuration is used Antenna radiation pattern: based on SLS EVM (from Agenda 10.1) \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | Study and identify set of essential information, if any, that is required to be conveyed as part of random access Msg 1/Msg 3/MsgA, respectively.<br>- Consider the RAN2 LS in R1-2601765 (R2-2601354)<br>Study mechanisms of conveying identified essential information | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | Study coverage enhancement mechanics and identify essential requirements, features, and components to support random access coverage requirements, including use cases, deployment scenarios for coverage enhancements.<br>Note: Moderator summary R1-2605052 Section 4.14 contains information of PRACH sequence/preamble format proposals from companies. Companies are encouraged to review the information for future evaluations. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
