| Agreement | For initial access, one configuration of one CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring is provided in MIB. The determination of the frequency location and bandwidth of the CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring is independent of UE’s bandwidth capability | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Conclusion | The frequency location and bandwidth of the SIB1-PDSCH refer to the schedulable frequency resources for SIB1-PDSCH | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Agreement | For initial access, study the following options for the frequency location and bandwidth of SIB1-PDSCH:<br>- Option 1: The frequency location and bandwidth of SIB1-PDSCH are the same as the frequency location and bandwidth of the CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring.<br>- Option 2: The frequency location and bandwidth of SIB1-PDSCH can be different from the frequency location and bandwidth of the CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Working Assumption | For initial access, the frequency location and bandwidth of the SIB1-PDSCH are determined independently of the UE’s bandwidth capability. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Agreement | - For 6GR PDCCH, a Resource Element Group (REG) consists of 1 RB in the frequency domain and one OFDM symbol in the time domain. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - RAN1 to confirm the working assumption on REG bundle<br>- REG bundle is the minimal granularity for PDCCH precoding<br>- REG bundle is used as at least as the unit for interleaving for interleaved CCE-to-REG mapping<br>- FFS the number of REGs per REG bundle | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Both non-interleaved and interleaved CCE-to-REG mapping to be defined for 6GR PDCCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Working Assumption | - For a given 6GR CORESET, there is only one CCE-to-REG mapping scheme: either interleaved or non-interleaved. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Study whether to support precoding granularity larger than a single REG bundle | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - UE transparent MU-MIMO using non-orthogonal DMRS with a single-port PDCCH DMRS reception from UE perspective is considered as the benchmark MU-MIMO transmission scheme for the study<br>  - FFS the feasibility and necessity of specific enhancements (e.g., to the hash function, scrambling, etc.)<br>- Study whether it is necessary and feasible to consider:<br>  - 6GR PDCCH MU-MIMO with orthogonal multi-port DMRS transmission from the NW, assuming that UE is aware about the single port used for the PDCCH transmission dedicated to it,<br>  - and additional necessity and feasibility that UE is aware about interfering/other ports or not<br>- Study whether it is necessary and feasible to consider:<br>  - 6GR PDCCH SU-MIMO with multi-port DMRS transmission from the NW and multi-port DMRS reception from UE perspective<br>    - UE is aware about the ports/multiple layers used for the PDCCH transmission dedicated to it | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Study the maximum number of RBs in 6GR PDCCH CORESET. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - For the study of slot-level PDCCH monitoring consider:<br>  - Whether monitoring occasions are restricted to the first [X] OFDM symbols of a slot<br>  - Whether monitoring occasions can be present in any consecutive [Y] symbols in a slot | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Use the following NR PDCCH DMRS mapping pattern as the benchmark for evaluation:<br>  - 3 DMRS REs per REG<br>  - DMRS is present in every OFDM symbol carrying the PDCCH<br>- Study 6GR PDCCH DMRS mapping patterns compared to the benchmark<br>- Study whether PDCCH DMRS REs for a PDCCH candidate are transmitted only within the REGs constituting that candidate. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | Consider the 5G NR Length-31 Gold sequence as the baseline for 6GR PDCCH DMRS study and evaluations. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Consider the 5G NR PDCCH CRC scrambling (masking) procedure as a starting point for 6GR PDCCH study<br>- Consider the 5G NR PDCCH payload bit-level scrambling procedure as a starting point for 6GR PDCCH study | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - For the study of the necessity, feasibility, dynamic sharing of time-frequency resources of 5G and 6G PDCCH transmission for MRSS consider that 5G NR and 6GR CORESETs/Search Spaces can partially or fully overlap. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | For PDCCH blocking evaluation, consider, e.g.,<br>- The distribution of the ALs<br>- Number of candidates per ALs<br>- Number of UEs to be scheduled at a time<br>- CORESET(s) configurations<br>- Search space | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Study how to achieve efficient support for the coexistence of wideband and narrowband CORESETs | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | List of parameters and values for link-level evaluations of 6GR PDCCH:<br>\| # \| Parameters \| Values \|<br>\| Deployment/General configuration \| Deployment/General configuration \|  \|<br>\| 1 \| Carrier Frequency \| - 0.7 GHz FDD - 2 GHz FDD, - 4 GHz TDD - 7 GHz, TDD - 30 GHz, TDD \|<br>\| 2 \| SCS \| - 15 kHz for 0.7 and 2 GHz - 30 kHz for 4 GHz and 7 GHz - 120 kHz for 30 GHz \|<br>\| 3 \| System BW \| Optional, up to company to report, e.g., 5MHz, 20 MHz, 100 MHz 400 MHz \|<br>\| 4 \| Waveform \| CP-OFDM \|<br>\| 5 \| Number of BS antenna \| For TDL channels: - For 700MHz/2GHz, 2Tx chains as baseline. - For 4GHz/7GHz/30GHz, 4Tx chains as baseline For CDL: - Up to company to report and following general 6G evaluation assumptions Other values up to company to report and following general 6G evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| 6 \| Number of UE antenna \| For 700MHz/2GHz: - 2Rx, 1Rx For 4GHz/7GHz/30GHz: - 4Rx, 2Rx \|<br>\| 7 \| Receiver type \| - Baseline: MMSE - Optional: MMSE-IRC, R-ML \|<br>\| 8 \| Channel estimation \| - Baseline: Realistic - Optional: Ideal \|<br>\| 9 \| UE speed, kmph \| - Baseline: 3 km/h, 120 km/h - Optional: 30 km/h, 350 km/h, 500 km/h \|<br>\| 10 \| Channel model, Delay spread, ns \| - TDL-C 300 ns, TDL-A 30 ns, CDL-C - Other models, e.g., TDL-C 30 ns, CDL-A/D and delay spreads, e.g., 30 ns, 100 ns, 300 ns, 1000 ns up to company report \|<br>\| 11 \| Interference \| - Baseline: absent, - Other modelling up to company report, e.g., for MU-MIMO \|<br>\| Control Channel configuration \| Control Channel configuration \|  \|<br>\| 12 \| Number of symbols for CORESET \| - Baseline: 1, 2, 3 OFDM symbols, - Other values up to company report \|<br>\| 13 \| CORESET frequency allocation, PRBs \| Up to company report \|<br>\| 13 \| CCE-to-REG mapping type \| - Interleaved, - Non-interleaved \|<br>\| 14 \| REG bundle size, REGs \| Up to company report \|<br>\| 16 \| CCE size, REGs \| Up to company report \|<br>\| 17 \| Aggregation level \| - Baseline: 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 - Other values up to company report \|<br>\| 18 \| CRC bits \| - Baseline: 24 bits - Other up to company report \|<br>\| 19 \| Channel coding \| - Baseline: NR Polar - Other options up to company report \|<br>\| 20 \| Modulation \| - Baseline: QPSK - Other options up to company report \|<br>\| 21 \| DCI payload size (excluding CRC), bits \| Up to company report \|<br>\| 23 \| Number of repetitions \| - Baseline: no repetitions - Other values up to company report \|<br>\| 24 \| Target BLER \| - Baseline: 1% - Other values up to company report \|<br>\| 25 \| FFS Link adaptation \| - Baseline: no - Other schemes up to company report \|<br>\| DMRS and Precoding \| DMRS and Precoding \|  \|<br>\| 26 \| DMRS sequence \| - Baseline: 5G NR Length-31 Gold sequence - Other up to company report \|<br>\| 27 \| Freq. domain DMRS density/ DMRS mapping \| - Baseline: 3 DMRS REs per REG, DMRS is present in every OFDM symbol carrying the PDCCH - Other up to company report \|<br>\| 28 \| Number of DMRS ports \| - Baseline: 1 - Other up to company report \|<br>\| 29 \| Tx Diversity scheme/Precoder \| - Baseline: 1-port Precoder cycling - Other up to company report \|<br>\| 30 \| Precoding granularity \| Up to company report \|<br>\| 31 \| Channel estimation algorithm \| Up to company report \|<br>\| 33 \| Decoding algorithm \| Up to company report \|<br>\| Metrics \| Metrics \|  \|<br>\| 34 \| Used metrics \| - Default: BLER, - Other up to company to report \|<br>\| NOTE: The parameters and values are applicable to PDCCH transmitted within the CORESET. \| NOTE: The parameters and values are applicable to PDCCH transmitted within the CORESET. \| NOTE: The parameters and values are applicable to PDCCH transmitted within the CORESET. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Support DMRS-based PDSCH transmission with one DMRS port per layer, where the layers and DMRS ports are precoded using the same precoders<br>  - Note: Whether/how to additionally support multiple DMRS ports per layer and/or DMRS-free PDSCH transmission is a separate discussion | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - Support both SU-MIMO and MU-MIMO for PDSCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - For DMRS based on TD-OCC-X for a single-slot based PDSCH, the TD-OCC-X is applied to X consecutive symbols for DMRS<br>  - Consider the same spacing between every X DMRS symbols<br>    - Study how to identify the spacing between every X DMRS symbols<br>    - FFS: whether there is any exception<br>  - The number of DMRS symbols in a slot is L*X, and study the candidate value of L as follows<br>    - For X = 1, L = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6}<br>    - For X = 2, L = {1, 2, 3}<br>    - For X = 4, L = {1}<br>    - If multiple candidate values of L are supported, study how to determine the value of L<br>    - Note: Above does not mean X = {1, 2, 4} are all supported<br>  - Study at least the following options for the time-domain location of the first DMRS symbol<br>    - Option 1: The first DMRS symbol is fixed as the first symbol allocated for the PDSCH<br>    - Option 2: The candidate DMRS symbol(s) are fixed in a slot and the actual first DMRS symbol is the first candidate DMRS symbol within the TDRA for the PDSCH<br>    - Option 3: The first DMRS symbol is indicated by the network relative to the first symbol of the PDSCH | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - For low overhead DMRS with AI/ML receiver for PDSCH reception, reuse the LLS EVM for PDSCH and RS agreed in RAN1 #124b<br>  - Support the following additional EVM for generalization/scalability of the AI/ML models across one or multiple of the followings<br>    - Various channel models, e.g., various CDL models<br>    - Various delay spread<br>    - Various Doppler spread<br>    - Various SINR<br>    - Various carrier frequencies<br>    - Various UE speed<br>    - Various UE antenna operations, e.g., UE selected antennas for DL reception, number of UE antennas, etc.<br>    - Various aNB antenna configurations, e.g., antenna spacing, TXRU mapping, antenna layout, etc.<br>    - Various downlink transmission operations, e.g., TDRA, FDRA, MCS, DMRS antenna port(s), PRG size, precoders, etc.<br>    - Various number of MIMO layers<br>  - Companies shall report the number of model parameters, AI/ML receiver related aspects (including at least model input, model output, and label) for the AI/ML model<br>    - Companies shall report the number of FLOPs, FLOPs per RB/slot/layer, together with the corresponding inference configuration (e.g., number of Rx antennas, number of subcarriers, number of symbols and number of layers for PDSCH)<br>    - Companies can optionally report whether/how the labelling error is modelled<br>  - The following model generalization aspects defined in TR38.843 can be further considered<br>    - Case 1: The AI model is trained based on training dataset from one Scenario #A/Configuration #A, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a dataset from the same Scenario #A/Configuration #A.<br>    - Case 2: The AI model is trained based on training dataset from one Scenario #A/Configuration #A, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a different dataset than Scenario #A/Configuration #A, e.g., Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B.<br>    - Case 3: The AI model is trained based on training dataset constructed by mixing datasets from multiple scenarios/configurations including Scenario #A/Configuration #A and a different dataset than Scenario #A/Configuration #A, e.g., Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a dataset from a single Scenario/Configuration from the multiple scenarios/configurations, e.g., Scenario #A/Configuration #A, Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - Study multi-TRP transmission for PDSCH(s), considering, at least, the following aspects:<br>  - Whether/how to support single PDSCH from a single TRP and/or from multiple TRPs, and/or multiple PDSCHs from multiple TRPs<br>  - Strive to minimize the number of multi-TRP transmission schemes<br>    - E.g., avoid multiple schemes targeting the same scenario<br>  - Whether/how to design common framework for both single-TRP and multi-TRP transmission<br>  - Study of multi-TRP transmission to include identifying deployment conditions and operating regimes where multi-TRP transmission provides performance/energy saving benefits relative to single-TRP transmission<br>    - E.g., high-speed train (HST) scenario | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - For frequency interleaving of PDSCH:<br>  - Support non-interleaved frequency mapping<br>  - Study interleaved frequency mapping, considering, at least, the following aspects:<br>    - Applicability to one or both of RIV-based and RBG-based mapping<br>    - Mapping granularity<br>    - Number of scheduled RBs<br>    - Whether the interleaving is within one of the following:<br>      - Subset or all of scheduled/allocated RB(s)<br>      - Configured/predefined interleaving bandwidth(s)<br>      - Subset or all of scheduled/allocated RB(s) within a configured/predefined interleaving bandwidth(s)<br>      - FFS: Definition of interleaving bandwidth(s)<br>      - FFS: Whether configured/predefined interleaving bandwidth(s) is independent of scheduled/allocated RB(s)<br>    - Impact on performance compared to non-interleaved frequency mapping and compared to NR interleaved frequency mapping<br>    - Impact on latency<br>    - Impact on multiplexing/scheduling<br>    - Impact on transmitter/receiver complexity | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - For precoding granularity of PDSCH on a single physical carrier:<br>  - Support PRB bundling, where UE assumes that precoding granularity is N consecutive PRBs in the frequency domain, and UE may assume the same precoding is applied within the precoding granularity<br>    - Note: How precoding is performed within the precoding granularity is up to aNB implementation<br>  - Further study which PRB bundling sizes, i.e., N, should be supported, considering, at least, support for wider bandwidth (more than 275 RBs), DMRS design, and UE-specific/common PDSCH transmission<br>    - PRB bundling sizes supported in NR as starting point for study<br>  - Further study potential impact (if any) of 2 RF chains at the UE for 400 MHz CBW<br>  - Further study whether PRB bundling size is layer-common or layer-specific<br>  - Note: Decision to study PRB bundling for the case of scheduling across multiple physical carriers is subject to agreements in other agenda items<br>  - FFS: Per subcarrier/PRB precoding granularity | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - For FDRA for PDSCH on a single physical carrier:<br>  - For RBG-based mapping:<br>    - Support nominal RBG size P larger than 16 RBs<br>    - Further study specific values for nominal RBG size<br>    - Further study whether nominal RBG size is configurable | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - For FDRA for PDSCH on a single physical carrier:<br>  - For RIV-based mapping:<br>    - Minimum granularity is 1 RB<br>    - Further study maximum granularity<br>    - Further study whether granularity is configurable | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - For PDSCH rate matching:<br>  - Study whether/how to support semi-static configuration and/or dynamic indication of rate matching pattern<br>  - Study whether/how to support RB-level, RE-level, and/or symbol-level  rate matching pattern<br>  - Study whether/how to support channel/signal-based rate matching pattern<br>  - Consider the following aspects for the study:<br>    - Which 6G channels and signals to consider for rate matching purposes<br>    - Which NR channels and signals to consider for rate matching purposes for 6GR-NR MRSS<br>    - Which NB-IoT/eMTC channels and signals to consider for rate matching purposes of the following RAN requirement in TR 38.914: “The 6GR shall support coexistence with NB-IoT (all deployment modes) and eMTC via semi-static configuration”<br>  - Note: Other aspects of 6GR-NR MRSS and 6G coexistence with NB-IoT/eMTC are discussed separately from PDSCH rate matching | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - Support single-TRP transmission for PDSCH, i.e., A single TRP serves the UE | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | - For study of multi-TRP transmission for PDSCH(s):<br>  - Consider a set of coordinated TRPs, which includes the set of serving TRP(s) and interference-coordination TRPs<br>  - Further study size of set of coordinated TRPs, and how to coordinate, if necessary | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| Agreement | Adopt the following LLS assumptions for evaluating UCI-dedicated physical channels:<br>\| Parameters \| Proposals \|<br>\| #1 Carrier frequency/Duplex \| Refer to the assumption for PUSCH and non-AI DMRS \|<br>\| #2 SCS \| Refer to the assumption for PUSCH and non-AI DMRS \|<br>\| #4 Channel model \| Refer to the assumption for PUSCH and non-AI DMRS \|<br>\| #5 Delay spread \| Refer to the assumption for PUSCH and non-AI DMRS \|<br>\| #6 UE speed \| Refer to the assumption for PUSCH and non-AI DMRS \|<br>\| #7 Antenna configuration \| Refer to the assumption for PUSCH and non-AI DMRS \|<br>\| #8 UCI payload \| 1/2/3/4/6/7/11/22/50/100 bits as reference, other values can also be evaluated \|<br>\| #9 PRBs \| 1, Other values can also be evaluated based on companies’ report \|<br>\| #10 OFDM symbols \| 1, 2, 4, 7, 14 Other values can also be evaluated based on companies’ report \|<br>\| #11 Format/TX scheme \| Companies to report, including the details \|<br>\| #12 Number of repetitions \| Companies to report \|<br>\| #13 Frequency hopping \| Companies to report \|<br>Chair’s guidance<br>The following address how to handle overlap in studies between Agenda Items 10.3.1 and 10.5.2.3 regarding transmission of 3—11 bits on the UCI-dedicated physical channel:<br>Case 1 (w/ channel coding): 3—11 bits  channel coding (in 10.3.1)  mapping  transmission.<br>Case 2 (w/o channel coding): 3—11 bits  mapping to sequence  transmission<br>How to compare Case 1 and Case 2:<br>- 10.3.1 works on channel coding for 3—11<br>- 10.5.2.3 works on transmission schemes outside of channel coding<br>  - For studying sequence based transmission<br>    - The baseline for comparisons is NR RM block code.<br>    - If a change to the NR RM block code or a new channel code for 3—11 bits is adopted in 10.3.1, it is also included in the comparison<br>Note: the lower and upper bound for UCI size in this study is discussed in 10.5.2.3. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For evaluation of UCI-dedicated physical channels, consider at least (but not limited to) the following metrics:<br>- For transmission scheme(s) with channel coding based UCI bit conveying with CRC,<br>  - BLER <=1%<br>  - NACK to ACK probability <=0.1%<br>- For transmission scheme(s) with channel coding based UCI bit conveying without CRC, and with sequence based UCI bit conveying,<br>  - BLER <=1% for UCI other than HARQ-ACK for comparison with transmission scheme(s) with channel coding based UCI bit conveying with CRC<br>  - BER <=1% for UCI other than HARQ-ACK for comparison among transmission scheme(s) with channel coding based UCI bit conveying without CRC, and with sequence based UCI bit conveying<br>  - DTX to ACK probability <=1%<br>  - ACK missed probability <=1%<br>  - NACK to ACK probability <=0.1%<br>- Receiver complexity at BS side, e.g., computational complexity, and UE TX transmission complexity, PAPR | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For the study of time domain resource allocation for PUSCH across more than one slot, consider:<br>- At least the following scenario(s):<br>  - Coverage improvements<br>  - Flexible usage of UL symbols in TDD<br>  - Overhead reduction in terms of the number of UL-scheduling DCIs<br>- At least the following scheme(s)<br>  - PUSCH repetition across multiple slots with the same starting symbol and length (a.k.a. repetition Type A in 5G NR)<br>  - PUSCH repetition across multiple slots with same/different starting symbols and/or length (a.k.a. repetition Type B in 5G NR)<br>  - Single TB generation over multiple slots (a.k.a. TBoMS in 5G NR)<br>  - A PUSCH allocated across slot boundary(ies)<br>- At least the following aspects<br>  - the maximum PUSCH duration<br>  - whether the single PUSCH transmission can occupy contiguous or non-contiguous symbols<br>  - impact on performance and complexity including UE processing granularity/timeline<br>  - maximum TB size limit<br>Note: whether/how to schedule multiple PUSCH transmissions using a single DCI is up to DCI content discussion | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR PUSCH transmission, to decide the supported maximum number of orthogonal DMRS ports, at least considering the followings:<br>- Option 1. Up to 24<br>- Option 2. Up to 32<br>- Option 3. Up to 48<br>- Option 4. Up to 96<br>- Motivation, justification, potential use case, scenario<br>- Company to provide at least the SLS results<br>  - LLS results can be optionally provided by company | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR PUSCH transmission, study DMRS design at least considering the following(s):<br>- DMRS time/frequency domain parameters<br>  - DMRS RE mapping<br>  - Number of CDM groups<br>  - Time domain DMRS symbol allocation<br>  - Length of TD/FD-OCC<br>  - Sequence of TD/FD-OCC<br>- Possibly common DMRS design for CP-OFDM and DFT-S-OFDM<br>- Possibly common DMRS design between PDSCH and PUSCH<br>- Possibly common DMRS design for between different receiver types (e.g., AI-based, non-AI based) | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR PUSCH transmission, study DMRS power boosting method, at least considering the following(s)<br>- NR DMRS power boosting can be a starting point, i.e., by indicating number of CDM group(s) without data<br>- PAPR difference, if any, between DMRS and PUSCH, at least impact on MU-MIMO performance<br>- PAPR/PSD of DMRS and PUSCH<br>- Different modulation order(s)<br>- Impact from/on DMRS design including different DMRS density in frequency domain<br>- When mature, send an LS to RAN4 to ask on the maximum PUSCH DMRS power boosting | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | Study whether/how to support closed-loop frequency selective precoding based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, considering at least following:<br>- Wideband precoding as baseline for evaluation purpose<br>- Applicable number of UE antenna port(s) and rank(s)<br>- Applicable waveform(s)<br>- Factors impacted on performance and complexity in UE/NW<br>  - Frequency selective precoding granularity<br>  - PAPR<br>  - Realistic channel estimation<br>  - UE antenna model<br>  - SRS sounding overhead & channel aging<br>  - Link budget<br>  - UL Link adaptation<br>  - UE TX coherency<br>  - PUSCH frequency resource allocation per UE<br>  - Number of BS RX antennas<br>  - UL precoder design and indication method with signaling overhead & potential overhead reduction<br>  - Other factors are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR PUSCH transmission<br>- Study codeword-to-layer mapping scheme at least considering the followings:<br>  - The number of codewords for each rank<br>  - The number of layer(s) per codeword for each rank<br>  - The maximum number of codewords<br>  - Mapping of same or different modulation order for a number of layer(s) of a codeword<br>  - Performance comparison with system aspects (e.g., DL control signaling overhead, HARQ complexity, different precoding schemes, impact on BS receiver implementation, performance with link adaptation and scheduling, small packet transmission with higher ranks, performance with directional antennas and/or impairments, MPR, antenna imbalance, UE complexity, processing delay, UL CSI acquisition, etc. Other aspects are not precluded)<br>- Study whether/how to map one or more transport block(s) to time/frequency resources and its impact on codeword-to-layer mapping<br>- Study same or different power allocation across-layers and its impact on codeword-to-layer mapping<br>- Study aspects related to modulation order and code rate and its impact on codeword-to-layer mapping | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR PUSCH transmission, study AI based receiver for PUSCH DMRS overhead reduction at least considering the followings:<br>- Study sub-case A (sparse DMRS) at least the following<br>  - AI receiver assumption for evaluation purpose<br>    - Including model input, output, and label<br>  - Label quality impact<br>  - Generalization and scalability performance<br>  - Model inference complexity and number of parameters<br>  - Data collection and performance monitoring<br>  - Relevant specification impact(s)<br>  - Other study aspects are not precluded<br>  - FFS: Whether to have same/different design for DFTS-OFDM and CP-OFDM, e.g., data/DMRS multiplexing in FDM manner, and PAPR<br>- Study sub-case B (super-imposed DMRS) and/or sub-case C (DMRS-free),<br>  - Consider how to address at least the following challenge aspects<br>    - Inter-layer interference, inter-UE interference, and inter-cell interference handling<br>    - PAPR<br>    - Whether/how to perform Rnn estimation<br>    - UE Coexistence<br>    - (For sub-case B) power allocation between DMRS and data<br>    - (For sub-case C) Dependency on the modulation design<br>    - (For sub-case C) inter-vendor collaboration issue, if any<br>    - Other challenge aspects are not precluded<br>  - Study aspects listed under sub-case A (sparse DMRS)<br>    - Other study aspects are not precluded<br>- Note: RAN1 strives to down-select up to 1 sub-case | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For the assumption of LLS for evaluation of AI based receiver for PUSCH DMRS overhead reduction, the agreed template in RAN1#124bis is updated as follows:<br>\| Parameters \| Value \|<br>\| #1 Receiver assumptions \| Reported up to company, for example, Details on Receiver options (in R1-2509598) Option 1: AI-based denoise on DMRS REs only Option 2: AI-based channel estimation for data REs (with or without denoise on DMRS REs) Option 3: AI based channel prediction for target REs Option 4: AI-based joint {channel estimation + equalization + demodulation/LLR calculation} Option 5: AI-based {channel estimation} + AI-based {equalization + demodulation/LLR calculation} Additional Receiver options Option 6: Channel estimation + equalization + AI-based {demodulation/LLR calculation} Option 7: AI-based {Channel estimation} + equalization + AI-based {demodulation/LLR calculation} Other receiver options are not precluded Note: Receiver type (such as AI/non-AI) including the above Options in aNB will not be specified. \|<br>\| #2 Model input \| Reported by company, including input content and input dimension \|<br>\| #3 Model output \| Reported by company, including output content and output dimension \|<br>\| #4 Label \| Reported by company, including label acquisition method (Labelling error model can be reported optionally) \|<br>\| #5 Training type \| Offline \|<br>\| #6 Number of MIMO layers \| Reported by company \|<br>\| #7 Number of FLOPs \| Reported by company Company also reports Inference related parameters for PUSCH (e.g., number of RBs, symbols) (Number of FLOPs per RB/slot/layer can be reported optionally) \|<br>\| #8 Number of parameters of AI Model \| Reported by company \|<br>\| #9 Benchmark \| Receiver: non-AI based receiver with realistic assumption Other aspects (including DMRS patterns, etc) can be reported by company \|<br>\| #9-1 DMRS assumption for AI receiver \| reported by company \|<br>\| #10 Aspects to consider for generalization and scalability performance \| Evaluation Methodologies & Frameworks Evaluation cases 1, 2, 3 in TR 38.843 Case 1: The AI model is trained based on training dataset from one Scenario #A/Configuration #A, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a dataset from the same Scenario #A/Configuration #A. Case 2: The AI model is trained based on training dataset from one Scenario #A/Configuration #A, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a different dataset than Scenario #A/Configuration #A, e.g., Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B. Case 3: The AI model is trained based on training dataset constructed by mixing datasets from multiple scenarios/configurations including Scenario #A/Configuration #A and a different dataset than Scenario #A/Configuration #A, e.g., Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a dataset from a single Scenario/Configuration from the multiple scenarios/configurations, e.g., Scenario #A/Configuration #A, Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B. Companies to report the scenarios/configurations for generalization and scalability verification. Channel generation: considering Link Level channel and/or System Level channel (e.g., UMi, UMa) Link Level channel, if used for generating training data, shall be generated with diversified Channel & Environmental Conditions Companies can also use System Level channel to generate training data set Channel & Environmental Conditions Various Channel Types Various Channel parameters, such as various Delay Spread, various Doppler spread, various Angular Distributions Various UE speed Various SNR range Various interference level FFS: how to model interference in LLS Scheduling & System Configurations Different numbers of Tx/Rx antenna ports, MIMO layers, SU/MU-MIMO scenario, and UL precoders (TPMI) Varying time/frequency resource allocation Different MCS & Modulation, if applicable Different DMRS Configurations: DMRS pattern, sequence, time/frequency domain density Power allocation (for Sub-case B) Other aspects are not precluded, and reported by company (if needed) \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | Study DL/UL reciprocity based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, considering at least following candidate schemes:<br>- Scheme 1. CSI-RS based DL channel estimation by UE, UE calculates UL precoder, UE transmits (precoded) SRS, and NW indicates UE precoder information to schedule (e.g., analogous with non-codebook based PUSCH in NR)<br>- Scheme 2. CSI-RS-based DL channel acquisition by UE, UE reports channel information to NW, and NW selects an UL precoder to schedule UE<br>  - FFS: What channel information UE reports<br>  - FFS: how to determine UL precoder<br>  - FFS: UL link adaptation<br>- Scheme 3. CSI-RS-based DL channel acquisition by UE, UE reports channel information to NW and/or send SRS, the NW indicates the information on the desired UL precoder, the UE calculates/selects the precoder<br>  - FFS: What channel information UE reports<br>  - FFS: How NW indicates the information on the desired UL precoder<br>  - FFS: How to represent the information<br>  - FFS: How the UE calculates/selects the precoder.<br>- Other scheme(s) are not precluded.<br>- Note: For any scheme(s) requiring DL/UL reciprocity at UE side, the feasibility of DL/UL reciprocity at UE side should be considered.<br>- Potential benefit(s) & scenario(s) over codebook-based PUSCH transmission as baseline<br>For the purpose of performance evaluation of DL/UL reciprocity based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, consider the following antenna calibration error:<br>- Antenna calibration error can be modeled as<br>  - represents the uplink channel obtained using DL/UL reciprocity at UE side and has a dimension .<br>  - is the downlink channel derived by CSI-RS and has a dimension .<br>  - is the number of antenna ports of UE,  is the number antenna ports of aNB.<br>  - is the amplitude mismatch coefficient and could be modeled as subject to uniform or Gaussian distribution, e.g., Gaussian distribution with zero mean and variance X1=0.35 dB.<br>  - is the phase mismatch coefficient and could be modeled as subject to uniform or Gaussian distribution, e.g., Gaussian distribution with zero mean and variance Y1=5 degrees.<br>  - Other values on X1 and Y1 can be considered and reported by companies.<br>  - is the amplitude mismatch coefficient and could be modeled as subject to uniform or Gaussian distribution, e.g., Gaussian distribution with zero mean and variance X2 dB.<br>  - is the phase mismatch coefficient and could be modeled as subject to uniform or Gaussian distribution, e.g., Gaussian distribution with zero mean and variance Y2 degrees.<br>  - Values on X2 and Y2 are reported by companies.<br>  - The distribution of  and  can also be reported by companies.<br>  - Both or either of aNB or UE-side antenna calibration error can be used in the simulation and is reported by companies.<br>  - It is assumed that DL/UL reciprocity calibration error is constant in UL scheduled bandwidth.<br>  - Companies report how DL/UL reciprocity calibration error behavior in time domain considering UL coherency is modeled.<br>- Send LS to RAN4 to inform the above contents and ask for feedback on model assumption above, whether DL/UL reciprocity can be achieved at UE side considering different UE implementations<br>- RAN1 continue to study, and after RAN1 sensitivity check, send an LS to RAN4 to inform the above and the outcome, with potential assessment/check from RAN4 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |

| Agreement | Study the adaptation of the number of Rx antennas for PDCCH monitoring in 6GR for UE energy efficiency purpose, including at least energy saving gain, impact to coverage, impact to PDCCH blocking, spec impacts, how to ensure common understanding between UE and NW, application delay of antenna adaptation, impact to PDSCH reception. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603879](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603879.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| Agreement | Study at least the following mechanisms for DCI Container size determinations at least for RRC connected state<br>- A DCI Container (defined for discussion purpose) is the physical payload that the UE attempts for decoding with a given size<br>  - no size alignment procedures across different DCI containers<br>- Alt 1: The DCI container size is implicitly determined by RRC for the DCI container, e.g., possible contents of the DCI<br>- Alt 2: The network explicitly configures DCI container size(s) via RRC<br>- Alt 3: DCI container size is pre-defined without any dependency on RRC<br>Note: the above studies are applicable for single-stage DCI and two-stage DCI<br>FFS: additional alternatives applicable for two-stage DCI only<br>FFS: maximum number of DCI container sizes for a UE<br>FFS: whether/how to do padding/truncation within a DCI container | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603880](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603880.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| Agreement | For the study of two-stage DCI in 6GR, consider at least the following options for the physical channels/signals for the two stages:<br>- Option 1: PDCCH for both the 1st stage and 2nd stage(s), and the 2nd stage(s) use the same transmission scheme and physical resource structure as the 1st stage<br>- Option 2: PDCCH for the 1st stage,<br>  - Dedicated time/frequency domain resource for the 2nd stage(s) is indicated in the 1st stage<br>  - The 2nd stage(s) use a different transmission scheme and/or physical resource structure from the 1st stage<br>    - FFS the physical channel for the 2nd stage and how the 2nd stage is transmitted over the indicated resource<br>- Option 3: PDCCH for the 1st stage, and<br>  - If the [2nd stage/two-stage] DCI schedules PDSCH(s) carrying DL-SCH data,<br>    - 2nd stage is multiplexed on PDSCH(s) with DL-SCH data<br>      - The 1st stage indicates the time/frequency domain resource for the PDSCH(s) with DL-SCH data<br>      - FFS how the multiplexing is done<br>  - If the [2nd stage/two-stage] DCI does not schedule any PDSCH carrying DL-SCH data,<br>    - Option 3-1: Same as Option 1<br>    - Option 3-2: Same as Option 2<br>- Option 4: sequence for the 1st stage, PDCCH for the 2nd stage<br>  - Note: this is different from WUS-based PDCCH monitoring adaptation at least in the sense that whenever a two-stage DCI is transmitted, both the sequence and the 2nd stage PDCCH are always transmitted, i.e., there is a one-to-one correspondence between the 1st stage and 2nd stage.<br>  - Note: deprioritize the sequence design until more detailed design of Option 4 is available.<br>  - FFS: FAR and MDR target for the sequence<br>- Note: Polar coding is assumed except for the 1st stage in Option 4. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603881](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603881.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| Agreement | For the study of two-stage DCI in 6GR, consider the following options for the information carried in the two stages:<br>- Option A<br>  - 1st stage DCI<br>    - Carry only the information to decode the 2nd stage DCI<br>  - 2nd stage DCI<br>    - Carry scheduling info for PDSCH(s)/PUSCH(s) and/or other downlink control information (e.g., same as single stage DCI)<br>- Option B<br>  - 1st stage DCI<br>    - Carry the information to decode the 2nd stage DCI and part of {scheduling info for PDSCH(s)/PUSCH(s) and/or other downlink control information}<br>  - 2nd stage DCI<br>    - Carry additional {scheduling info for PDSCH(s)/PUSCH(s) and/or other downlink control information} on top of those in 1st stage DCI<br>- Option C<br>  - 1st stage: a sequence indicating at least the presence of the 2nd stage DCI and/or the information to decode the 2nd stage<br>  - 2nd stage DCI: same as a single-stage DCI<br>  - Note: this option is applicable to only Option 4 of the physical channels/signals for the two stages<br>- Other options are not precluded.<br>- Note: companies are encouraged to analyse pros/cons of above options compared with single stage DCI | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603881](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603881.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| Agreement | For the study on the ambiguity between DTX of DL assignment and PDSCH decoding failure in the HARQ-ACK codebook for 6GR, consider at least:<br>  - NW implementation based approach including impacts on UEs with different NW implementation if any, impact of DTX events (e.g., assuming PDCCH missed detection probability is 1%)<br>  - Self-decodable and non-self decodable PDSCH<br>  - Trade-off between additional feedback overhead and performance benefits | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604825](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604825.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | For DL HARQ in 6GR, support HARQ-ACK information bits transmitted via L1 signalling (i.e., Mechanism 1).<br>- The design of DL HARQ-ACK feedback operation allows at least the possibility for the following while ensuring HARQ-ACK performance<br>  - Relaxing the need for tight coordination between schedulers (e.g., between multiple carriers, between DL and UL, etc.)<br>    - Decoupling of HARQ-ACK feedback triggering from the DL assignments scheduling the corresponding PDSCH<br>    - Other solution that addresses the same issue can also be considered<br>  - Other factors can also be considered<br>- The design of DL HARQ-ACK feedback operation allows the simplification of UL channel collision/multiplexing/transmission (e.g., resource determination) involving HARQ-ACK | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604826](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604826.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | Study HARQ-ACK codebook(s) for downlink HARQ operation in 6GR, considering:<br>- HARQ-ACK codebook based on NR Type-1, NR Type-2, or NR (enhanced) Type-3 as starting point | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604827](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604827.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | In 6GR, for the study of downlink HARQ-ACK feedback granularity smaller than TB-level and corresponding mechanism, consider at least the following:<br>- XOR-based retransmission feedback<br>- CB/CBG-level HARQ-ACK feedback of high-probability error patterns<br>- NR CBG HARQ-ACK feedback<br>- Codebook-based feedback<br>- Two-stage feedback<br>- 2D lattice-based CBs feedback<br>- Per-PDSCH on demand request of CBG-level feedback<br>- Mixture of TB-level and CBG-level feedback<br>- Non-uniform CB-to-CBG mapping feedback<br>- HARQ-ACK feedback enabling/disabling for some of CBGs<br>- enabling/disabling of CBG-level HARQ-ACK feedback<br>- Separate HARQ-ACK for CBs that carry MAC CEs or similar important data<br>Note: in the above applicable options, CB-level and CBG-level can be both considered.<br>Note: For the evaluation of above options, consider at least (average) feedback overhead per TB, ratio between size of retransmitted CB(s)/CBG(s) and size of original TB, and impact on DL control overhead, considering practical setup (e.g., fading channel, correlation across CBs, link adaptation, etc.)<br>Note: Companies are encouraged to check FL summary and contributions | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604827](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604827.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |

| Agreement | RAN1 to evaluate the BSR in CB PUSCH, considering at least the following aspects:<br>- The performance of decoding a PUSCH payload when N UEs transmit in the one same time and frequency PUSCH resource with considering the following assumptions:<br>  - The N UEs use N different DMRS ports from a common pool of M (N<= M) different DMRS ports.<br>  - The N UEs use same DMRS port from a common pool of M (N <= M) different DMRS ports but with different scrambling IDs for DMRS sequence.<br>  - The N UEs use different combinations of DMRS port and scrambling ID for DMRS sequence, where the DMRS port(s) are chosen from a common pool of M(N<=M) different DMRS ports.<br>  - The N UEs could use same or different waveform for PUSCH transmission.<br>  - The N UEs use the same DMRS port from a common pool of M (N <= M) different DMRS ports and same scrambling IDs for DMRS sequence<br>- Evaluate the following aspects of CB-BSR:<br>  - Study and identify the definition of UE collision in CB PUSCH used for this evaluation and evaluate the probability of UE collision.<br>    - Companies to report the definition used in their evaluations<br>  - The L1 transmission delay of BSR in CB PUSCH.<br>    - The L1 transmission delay is the time duration between the traffic arrival and when the BSR is decoded successfully.<br>  - The CDF of the number of attempts of BSR transmission.<br>  - The complexity of blind detection at the BS side.<br>    - The evaluation can take into account that UEs uses C-RNTI or common RNTI for PUSCH scrambling and/or the number of different DMRS that is detected.<br>  - The UL resource overhead for transmitting CB BSR.<br>  - The impact of DMRS false alarm (if any).<br>  - The above study and evaluation can consider system load. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |

| Agreement | For UE-to-UE CLI measurement and reporting in 6GR:<br>- RAN1 to study the following metrics for UE-to-UE CLI measurements:<br>  - RSSI and RSRP for direct interference, e.g., intra-band or intra-subband CLI.<br>  - RSSI to measure the leakage of the interference, e.g., inter-subband CLI.<br>  - The frequency domain granularity for measurement to be considered are: subband and wideband.<br>- RAN1 to study the following methods of CLI measurement resources:<br>  - CLI measurement based on explicit configuration of CLI RSSI measurement resource and/or SRS for RSRP measurement.<br>  - CLI measurement/detection without explicit measurement resource configuration, e.g., exploit PDSCH reception to measure/detect CLI.<br>- Study network-controlled CLI reporting.<br>  - The benefits/drawbacks of network-controlled CLI reporting.<br>  - The reporting signalling and triggering mechanisms.<br>  - Study the following candidate reporting contents:<br>    - Option 1: N strongest RSRP or RSSI and corresponding resource indicators.<br>      - Report RSRP/RSSI for the strongest one and differential RSRP/RSSI for the other N-1.<br>    - Option 2: indication of whether each measurement resource has RSRP or RSSI measurement larger than a threshold, for example one bitmap with each bit corresponding to one measurement resource.<br>    - and any other options.<br>- Study UE-initiated/event-triggering CLI reporting for overhead reduction and energy saving, at least considering the following aspects:<br>  - The benefits over the network-controlled reporting.<br>  - The definition of CLI event and study how to trigger the CLI reporting.<br>  - The measurement resource/occasions for UE to detect the CLI event.<br>  - The reporting contents and the UL reporting mechanism.<br>- For the time alignment issue of CLI measurement resource:<br>  - Study the reference timing that is used for each associated measurement occasions.<br>  - Study whether/how the time alignment issue has impact on CLI measurement performance.<br>  - Study the methods to handle the time alignment, if necessary | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | For BS-to-BS CLI measurement in 6GR:<br>- RAN1 recommends to use CSI-RS and synchronization signal (e.g., 6GR SSB) to measure BS-to-BS CLI or Channel.<br>  - Further study other channel or RS used for measuring BS-to-BS CLI.<br>- Study UL resource muting mechanism for handling BS-to-BS in 6GR<br>  - The UL resource muting mechanism specified in 5G/NR is the baseline for study.<br>  - Study both transparent and non-transparent UL resource muting mechanisms,<br>  - Study the mechanism with flexible UL resource muting symbol position(s) and flexible disable/enable indication.<br>  - Study UL resource muting pattern.<br>- Study the information exchange between the BSs, at least including the configuration of the CSI-RS and synchronization signal, measurement results, the recommended beam index and non-recommended beam index, the TDD time/frequency resources configuration, the SBFD time/frequency resources configuration. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |

| Agreement | Study at least the following sequence families for 6GR DL WUS design,<br>- Zadoff-Chu sequences, or its variants<br>- m-sequences, or its variants<br>- Gold sequences, or its variants<br>at least with respect to,<br>- Link level performance (MDR, FAR, FDR), including time and frequency errors<br>- Maximum number of available OFDM sequences for carrying information,<br>- Sequence lengths and T/F mapping,<br>- UE/BS complexity and energy consumption,<br>- Overhead,<br>- Co-existence with other 6GR signals and channels,<br>Other aspects are not precluded.<br>R2-2605130	Summary #2 of DL WUS design	Moderator (Ericsson)<br>R2-2605063	Summary #2 of DL WUS design	Moderator (Ericsson)<br>R2-2605062	Summary #1 of DL WUS design	Moderator (Ericsson) | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| Agreement | For the paging rate per PO for evaluation purpose, assume per UE paging rate as RE, REF= 1%, 0.1%, 0.01% or 0.001% and YREF = 1.28s, and group paging rate RG can be determined as below:<br>-	RG = 1 – (1 – RE)N, where N is the number of UEs in the group<br>-	FFS: Value of N<br>Note:<br>-	For i-DRX with cycle duration Y second,<br>-	Per UE paging probability RE = 1 – (1 – RE, REF )Y/YREF<br>-	For e-DRX with K i-DRX cycles duration, PTW duration of L i-DRX cycles, and an i-DRX cycle duration Y second<br>-	Per UE paging probability is<br>-	RE = 1 – (1 – RE, REF )(K-L+1)Y/YREF for the first i-DRX cycle within the PTW<br>-	RE = 1 – (1 – RE, REF )Y/YREF for each of the remaining L-1 i-DRX cycles within the PTW | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | The relative power values of EE processing are applied to the serving cell RRM measurement by EE processing.<br>- Sequence related processing is assumed | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | Scale EE processing power of intra-frequency measurement only as below when no cell detection is assumed<br>\| N: Number of cells for intra-frequency measurement \| Synchronous case \|<br>\| N=8 \| 1.5x \|<br>\| N=4 \| 1.2x \|<br>- All above values are slot-averaged power<br>- Synchronous case means actual SSB transmissions from cells are time-aligned e.g., timing of SSB i from cell i is aligned with timing of SSB i from cell j.<br>- Sequence related processing is assumed<br>- N includes serving cell and neighbor cell(s)<br>- Note: Sleep power is not scaled by the table<br>- Note: values are for FR1, and  ~7GHz<br>- FFS relative power values of asynchronous case | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | For intra-frequency neighboring cell RRM measurement for RRC idle and RRC connected by EE processing, study at least<br>- RRM measurement by EE processing, including at least<br>- How to perform neighboring cell measurement, considering timeline and the number of neighboring cells<br>- Whether/how to limit the applicable cases: synchronous case only or both synchronous case and asynchronous case<br>- Cell detection/identification (if applicable), is performed by:<br>- Alt 1: EE processing, including at least<br>  - Study feasibility of<br>    - cell ID detetion, SSB index detetion if applicable<br>    - Note: Sequence related processing is assumed<br>  - How to perform neighboring cell detection/identification, considering timeline and the number of neighboring cells<br>  - Whether/how to limit the applicable cases: synchronous case only or both synchronous case and asynchronous case<br>- Alt 2: non-EE processing | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | At least the following assumptions are made for evaluating the achievable SNR and accuracy for serving cell RRM measurement for both RRC idle and RRC connected by EE processing.<br>\| Carrier frequency \| 3.5 GHz \|<br>\| WUS SCS \| 15kHz for 700MHz; 30 kHz for other frequencies \|<br>\| Channel model \| TDL-C, 300 ns delay spread \|<br>\| UE velocity \| 3km/h \|<br>\| PSS/SSS \| NR PSS/SSS \|<br>\| Impairment modelling \| TO before SSB for measurement: up to x us CFO before SSB for measurement: up to x ppm Note: the uniform distribution from -max to +max is assumed for TO and CFO in the evaluation \|<br>\| Number of Tx antennas \| 1 \|<br>\| Number of Rx antennas \| 1, 2 \|<br>\| Number of samples for measurement averaging \| 1, 2, 3, 4 \|<br>\| SNR \| [-6 dB, -7dB, -8 dB] FFS the SNR for ~7GHz \|<br>\| Inter-cell interference \| 3dB higher than measured cell. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |

| Agreement | For collection of UE energy saving gain results for 6GR DL WUS triggering PDCCH monitoring in RRC connected, companies are encouraged to consider the following cases:<br>- C-DRX<br>- DCP with C-DRX, UE monitors DCP during the X [slots/symbols] prior to the beginning of a slot where the UE would start the drx-onDurationTimer. X is up to companies’ report<br>- 6GR OFDM sequence based DL WUS with C-DRX, UE monitors 6GR DL WUS during the X [slots/symbols] prior to the beginning of a slot where the UE would start the drx-onDurationTimer. X is up to companies’ report<br>- 6GR OFDM sequence based DL WUS without C-DRX<br>- Note1: it’s up to companies’ report how the evaluated C-DRX different from NR C-DRX<br>- Note2: it’s up to companies’ report how the evaluated DCP different from NR DCP<br>- Note3: when comparing different cases, companies are encouraged to at least consider the same monitoring periodicity of 6GR DL WUS and C-DRX cycle. Other periodicities are up to companies’ report.<br>- Note4: Companies to report assumptions on measumrents | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | - For study of feasibility for UL WUS, at least followings are observed for DS#1c.<br>  - UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR SSB transmitted by the standalone cell.<br>  - UE can obtain information necessary for UL WUS transmission/operation and determine UL WUS transmission power at least via always-on 6GR SSB and periodic SIB1 and/or RRC signaling transmitted by the standalone cell. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | - For study of feasibility for UL WUS, at least followings are observed for DS#2c.<br>  - If UE transmits UL WUS to cell/carrier #1,<br>    - UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR SSB transmitted by cell/carrier #1.<br>    - UE can obtain information necessary for UL WUS transmission/operation and determine UL WUS transmission power at least via always-on 6GR SSB and periodic SIB1 and/or RRC signaling transmitted by cell/carrier #1.<br>  - If UE transmits UL WUS to cell/carrier #2,<br>    - UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR SSB transmitted by cell/carrier #2.<br>    - UE can obtain information necessary for UL WUS transmission/operation and determine UL WUS transmission power at least via always-on 6GR SSB and periodic SIB1 and/or RRC signaling transmitted by cell/carrier #2. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | - For study of feasibility for UL WUS, at least followings are observed for DS#2a.<br>  - If UE transmits UL WUS to cell/carrier #1,<br>    - UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR SSB transmitted by cell/carrier #1.<br>    - UE can determine UL WUS transmission power at least based on always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by cell/carrier #1 and information to determine UL WUS transmission power, when information is available at least from cell/carrier #1.<br>  - If UE transmits UL WUS to cell/carrier #2,<br>    - UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR SSB and/or periodic SIB1 transmitted by cell/carrier #1, if cell/carrier #1 and cell/carrier #2 are co-located and synchronized. Otherwise, it is required to further study how to acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization.<br>    - UE can determine UL WUS transmission power at least based on always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by cell/carrier #1 and information when available at least from cell/carrier #1, if cell/carrier #1 and cell/carrier #2 are co-located and synchronized. Otherwise, it is required to further study the feasibility and how to determine UL WUS transmission power and/or transmission beam.<br>- Scenario where UE moves from cell/carrier #1 to cell/carrier #2 (where cell/carrier #1 may not provide underlay coverage for cell/carrier #2) and can maintain DL timing/frequency synchronization and UL WUS configuration for a certain period of time is part of DS#2a<br>  - it is required to further study the feasibility and how to maintain DL timing/frequency synchronization and UL WUS configuration for a certain period of time.<br>- It is required to further study triggering conditions for UE to transmit UL WUS for cell/carrier #2, given that no DL signals/channels are available from cell/carrier #2.<br>- Note: Whether cell/carrier #1 and cell/carrier #2 are synchronized follows RAN4 definition. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | - For study of feasibility for UL WUS, at least followings are observed for DS#1a.<br>  - Majority companies expressed the concern that UE cannot discover the cell or UE cannot acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission even with the assistance of GNSS, while some companies claimed that GNSS (if applicable) can be used for GNSS-capable UE to derive timing information and acquire frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission.<br>  - Majority companies expressed the concern that UE cannot determine UL WUS transmission power without DL signals by the standalone cell, while some companies claimed that UE can use pre-configured or pre-defined parameters for UL power control for UL WUS transmission. Some companies indicated that pre-configured or pre-defined parameters for UL WUS transmission power may lead to excessive interference and BS/UE energy consumption. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR EE evaluation,<br>- BS power consumption is provided by<br>  - For ,<br>    - for BS in active DL transmission or UL reception where  is the BS dynamic power determined based on TR 38.864 and the scaling methods for BS for non-sleep mode in TR 38.864 clause 5.1 are reused, or<br>    - for BS in sleep mode<br>  - For ,<br>    - Option 1 or Option 2 (which option is taken is up to company) is applied for the case of reference configuration Set 4 and TRxRUs more than 64 and Option 1 is applied for the other cases.<br>    - Option 1:<br>      - is the relative power corresponding to the current sleep state and  for BS in active DL transmission or UL reception.<br>    - Option 2:<br>      - is {0.5, 0.8, 0.9, 0.99} or optionally (), is up to company report<br>      - is scaling factor calculated as:<br>        - for , , , , respectively.<br>        - is the fraction of (active) TRxRUs among all TRxRUs<br>      - is the relative power corresponding to the current sleep state of  fraction of TRxRUs, and  for BS in active DL transmission or UL reception.<br>      - is the relative power corresponding to the current sleep state of  fraction of TRxRUs<br>      - , ,<br>      - is the transition energy for  fraction of TXxRUs and  is the transition energy for  fraction of TXxRUs where  is the value of transition energy of the corresponding sleep state<br>      - Design of the 6GR specification’s signal and channel properties regarding, e.g., coverage, assumes the BS is using all of its TRxRUs. This does not mandate for BS to use all of its TRxRUs for UL WUS reception.<br>      - Note: This BS power consumption modeling for Option 2 is applicable at least to UL WUS reception<br>    - BS with reduced active TRxRUs, operates with reduced coverage, compared that BS uses all of its TRxRUs<br>    - FFS<br>      - UL WUS coverage target and impact on UE power consumption/complexity when BS operates with reduced active TRxRUs<br>- Transitions to and from sleep states assume a transition time partitioning of<br>  - Ramp-down: 30%, Ramp-up: 70%<br>- Note: This BS power consumption modeling is only for evaluation purpose. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR NTN, RAN1 to develop an SLS methodology for multiple satellites to evaluate PHY aspects of multi-satellite operation:<br>- FFS: How many satellites, including whether to simulate a whole constellation<br>- FFS: Which scenarios, e.g. single or multi-orbit<br>- NOTE: Unless explicitly agreed otherwise in future RAN1 meetings, it is assumed that the UE is served by one satellite at a time.<br>NOTE: Single-satellite SLS methodology will also be developed. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604768](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604768.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | For 6G NTN, RAN1 to evaluate solutions for satellite having more beam footprints than simultaneously active beams by reporting the following for a given combination of {hierarchical beam structure scheme, default SSB periodicity}:<br>- Coverage ratio for common channels, obtained as per Rel-19 methodology as (N2/(N1 + N2)) assuming N3=0.<br>- Resource overhead of common channels.<br>- Impact on link budget and cell acquisition latency (e.g. for a single sync raster point).<br>- Impact (from RAN1 perspective) on measurements<br>- NOTE: Details on assumptions for this evaluation (e.g. percentage of simultaneous active beams for UL and DL, calculation of cell acquisition latency, link budget, what common channels and their configuration are included for the resource overhead, etc.) will be separately discussed, and can be reported by companies until RAN1 can reach an agreement.<br>- Companies to report details on the proposed hierarchical beam structure scheme and default SSB periodicity, including at least:<br>  - Details on hierarchical beam structure scheme include at least the following:<br>    - Assumed beam structure (e.g. wide/narrow beam, including the assumed beamwidth for the different beams), for both UL and DL.<br>    - What channels / signals are transmitted in the different beams.<br>    - Peak EIRP / antenna gain of the different beams<br>    - Beam illumination pattern for the different beams (e.g. TDM between wide/narrow beam)<br>    - Methodology for calculation of N1/N2<br>  - Details on default SSB periodicity include at least the following:<br>    - Beam illumination scheme for different signals / channels.<br>    - SSB periodicity value.<br>- NOTE: The above does not imply that hierarchical beam structure is only relevant for cases where the satellite has more beam footprints than simultaneously active beams<br>- NOTE: Structure of NR signals / channels can be initially used for evaluation until other agenda items progress on the 6GR signals / channel structure.<br>NOTE: The Rel-19 agreement including the definition of N1/N2/N3 is as follows:<br>\| For system level study based on analytical evaluation: N1 beam footprints are in state “off” These beam footprints are not served by any signal (no satellite service in this area) N2 beam footprints are in state “common messages only” These beam footprints do not have any active user traffic, and are served the necessary information for cell discovery and initial access. Optionally, companies may consider user arrival (e.g. RACH access) in this type of cell, and should describe how this is taken into account in the analytical evaluation N3 beam footprints are in state “active traffic” These beam footprints have X active (e.g. VoNR) users each. These beam footprints are also served the necessary information for cell discovery and initial access N1 + N2 + N3 = “Total number of beam footprints “ N1, N2, N3, X are to be reported by companies. Resource utilization obtained under the assumptions above is to be reported by companies. Other assumptions made in the evaluation are to be reported by companies, e.g. power sharing scheme, beam hopping scheme, etc. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604768](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604768.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR NTN, RAN1 to agree link level evaluation assumptions for the link budget template at least for the following channels:<br>- PDCCH<br>- SSB<br>- PDSCH<br>- PUSCH<br>- PRACH<br>- PUCCH<br>NOTE: The purpose of link budget evaluation is to determine coverage gaps and margines.<br>NOTE: Structure of NR signals / channels can be initially used for evaluation until other agenda items progress on the 6GR signals / channel structure. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604768](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604768.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | At least the following parameters for satellite assistance info for UL time-frequency pre-compensation are supported in 6GR NTN:<br>- Ephemeris<br>- Epoch time<br>- Validity duration<br>- RAN1 to further discuss whether the following parameters are supported:<br>  - Parameter(s) to compensate common Doppler of the feeder link<br>  - Common TA<br>  - Common TA drift rate<br>  - Common TA drift rate variation<br>- FFS: details for each of the parameters<br>- Note: Use of the terminology “Ephemeris”, “Epoch time” and “Validity duration” does not imply that the solutions are the same as 5G NR-NTN. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604769](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604769.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | RAN1 to study the following techniques for k-offset in 6GR NTN:<br>- UE-specific k-offset.<br>- Update of broadcast k-offset over time (e.g. based on satellite location)<br>- For broadcast k-offset (which is agreed to be supported)<br>  - Beam specific k-offset<br>  - Cell specific k-offset | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604769](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604769.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
